# labdrian foreman role + OpenCode v2 runtime

Objective: run the labdrian harness on OpenCode v2.2.7 with the owner's ChatGPT (Codex) subscription, with the same experience as Claude Code, detached from gentle-ai; design one labdrian lead role, `foreman` (L6), that drives the whole labdrian workflow and is shared by Claude Code and OpenCode.
Branch: `feat/labdrian-orchestrator-opencode`, base `origin/main` 87f26495 (rebased in L15, reconciled in L16; first shaped on e25f0bfe). Delivery strategy: ask-on-risk (chain strategy not chosen yet). Test runner: `go test` in `engine/` (run with `TMPDIR` outside `/tmp`, see L3).

## Specs

- **S1 — Parity goal and scope.** The owner asked: "che podemos terminar de adaptar contratos skills hooks workflows agentes para opencode y para dsh deepseek harness", "asi como funcionan aca", "ppruqe necesito podes usar mi subcripcion de codex en un agente que sea mas decenre que el de codez", "quieeo algo que funcione como claude code con mi harness". Scope: OpenCode v2.2.7 only (L2.1 "si pero actualizalo a la ultima version", L2.2). Out of scope: DSH (discarded, L2.1), Pi customization, OpenCode 1.x ("saquemosla"). Acceptance: every piece of S2-S13 either works in OpenCode with test evidence or is declared unsupported with its reason in the capability declarations (S12); nothing is claimed that no test or recorded run shows.
- **S2 — foreman role source.** The owner: "aca en claude code no tenemos un orquestador tendriamos que diseñar uno  poruqe deberia ser mas parecido al.shaper que al build"; L5: "deberiamos llamarlo de alguna manera diferente que no se llame orchestador"; L6: the name is `foreman`. L2.4: it converses, clarifies, classifies, routes to roles, writes only the feature document, never source. L8: "para cambios chicos tenemos u a ruta ya ... lo otro es que quiero que mejoremos fuertemente y que se adapte a workflow de labdrian roles y hooks contratos": the foreman never edits source, not even for a small change, and the body is specific to labdrian's stages, roles, hooks and contracts (S3, S16). Deliverable: `roles/foreman/body.md`, designed by reverse-engineering the `<!-- gentle-ai:orchestrator -->` block of `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `skills/inception-pipeline`, `skills/role-chain`, the `labdrian` engine verbs and hooks and the owner's standing memory rules (keep/drop/change in L7, stage evidence in L9), then the minimalism ladder applied to wording, not to coverage (L8); software development only. Acceptance: the body is the L9 draft with the one cell L29 rewords, the Tier row's stop condition, which now names the `inception-pipeline` checklist rule it means. The body is about 45 lines (replacing the 13-line L7 draft; L8 lifts the v3 §2 line 44 length guide); it sends entry and closure to `inception-pipeline`, every hop to `role-chain` and the document format to `odd-feature-doc` and holds only what the foreman checks at each stage; a test fails when a `labdrian` verb or a skill the body names does not exist in the repository; it carries no gentle-ai review lifecycle, provider-defect handoff, Engram instruction or persona; `odd-feature-doc` gains a `foreman` row in its Decision Gates (derived from L2.4: the foreman writes the feature document); the agent id is `foreman` and no file outside this feature names the role "orchestrator". Assumption: `foreman` is not added to the closed eight-role chain vocabulary of `engine/roles` (`roles.go:18-25`); it is the parent the chain already names (`skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:13`).
- **S3 — foreman drives the whole labdrian workflow.** L4: "quiero que maneje todo el.workflow del labdrian overlay". Interpretation (the parent's reading, adopted by the shaper, not the owner's words): the foreman drives the lifecycle end to end by reusing what exists, never reimplementing it: entry and tier selection through `skills/inception-pipeline` (tier 1/2/3, producers, chain entry, closure-feedback), where a small change is Tier 3 (`skills/inception-pipeline/references/tier-selection.md:48-54`: lightweight requirements → `roadmap-maker` `incremental-insert` `tier: 3` → chain entry) and never an edit by the foreman (L8); the ODD stages authorize, explore, resolve-uncertainty, classify and track as `labdrian workflow open --feature F --task T` records them (`engine/cmd/usage.go:351-360`), then implement task by task and close; the `labdrian workflow` verbs create, bind, stage, verify, close, pause, resume and status (`usage.go:318-350`) where a step needs them; every hop of the chain (prototyper, shaper, estimator, builder, sweeper, polisher, reviewer, verifier, delivery) and the chained per-commit review through `skills/role-chain`; push, pull request and merge stay the owner's decisions. For each stage the body names the role it launches or the verb it runs, the gate or hook that answers (memory gate and paused-workflow deny of `projection hook`, `roles builder-guard`, `gate-task`, `review stop-hook`, `skills guard-hook`, `shaper guard-hook`), the evidence required before advancing, and when it stops for the owner (stage table and evidence in L9). Acceptance: the body names those two skills and the verbs and holds no step of theirs; no skill the foreman loads tells it to use Engram (accepted, L11): `skills/inception-pipeline` already uses memorycard on main (`SKILL.md:16`, `references/tier-selection.md:7`, L16), and `skills/procedural-candidates` moves to memorycard before T8 at all three of its layers (L16): the skill (`SKILL.md:17` `mem_session_summary`, `:19` Engram `mem_save`), its shared contract (`skills/_shared/procedural-candidate-detection.md:7-9,64-105`: candidate records and `engram:<id>` occurrence ids) and the `skills candidates` source (`engine/skills/engramexport/source.go` behind the `skills.CandidateSource` port, `engine/skills/candidate_source.go:15-18`), which a memorycard adapter behind the same port replaces, reading the `procedural/candidates/` and `procedural/drafts/` families through a new `memorycard list --topic-prefix --json` verb added in the memorycard repository (owner: "B", L35; verb contract and adapter in L37); legacy `engram:<id>` occurrence ids are translated to the memorycard id they were imported as, so that one observation listed as `engram:<n>` and as `memorycard:<m>` counts once (owner, L46: "traducelos"), through a read-only `memorycard resolve` verb added in the memorycard repository that reads its `import_map` (contract, unknown ids and engine port in L46): an id the import never mapped stays as written and counts as itself, and no stored record is rewritten; a recorded Claude Code run takes one small change from the owner's request through `workflow open`, builder, reviewer and delivery to the foreman's closure report (T8). Amended in L123 (owner, L121: "quiero mantener la regla del ci"; replaced by L122: "pero nada vamos con la 1", option 1 of the parent's question: "Agregar un camino para repos sin CI (lo que recomiendo). El foreman cierra con el commit de landing más los tests locales que registró delivery, y lo anota como `CI: none configured`. Sirve para el sandbox y para cualquier proyecto tuyo que no tenga CI. La contra es que hay que definir cómo se establece que un repo "no tiene CI", para que no sirva de excusa cuando el CI existe y falla."): closure in a repository with no CI. The engine, not the foreman, establishes "no CI": a read-only verb `labdrian review ci --landing <rev> --head <rev> [--cwd <repo>]` (name proposed, T46) exits 0 and prints one stdout line that starts `CI: none configured` only when all three hold: (1) the landing commit holds the delivered head (`IsAncestor`, `engine/review/app/app.go:144-146`); (2) `git remote` lists no remote; (3) the tree of the landing commit (`TreeFiles`, `app.go:129-131`: the commit, never the working tree) holds no CI marker. CI markers (assumption: this list, paths from the root of the tree only): any file under `.github/workflows/`, `.gitea/workflows/`, `.forgejo/workflows/`, `.buildkite/` or `.woodpecker/`, and the files `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `.circleci/config.yml`, `.travis.yml`, `azure-pipelines.yml`, `Jenkinsfile`, `bitbucket-pipelines.yml`, `.drone.yml`, `.woodpecker.yml`, `appveyor.yml` and `.appveyor.yml`. Every other case exits non-zero with one stderr line naming what it found (the markers, the remotes, or the head the landing does not hold) and saying that the no-CI closure does not apply; an unresolvable revision or a git failure is non-zero too, never `CI: none configured`. The verb writes nothing; its answer is reproducible from the commit and the repository's remotes. The foreman's Close row (`roles/foreman/body.md:29`) keeps "the merge commit, and CI read green" and gains one alternative: when that verb exits 0, the landing commit, its `CI: none configured` line and the local test results in delivery's entry; the foreman's closure report quotes the verb's line on its Evidence line (S16 B7), which is where `CI: none configured` is written. Delivery's entry holds those results: `roles/delivery/body.md` gains one sentence: run the test runner the feature document's header names at the delivered head and put the command and its exact result in the entry, or write it not run when the worktree is not at that head. Acceptance: T46's command tests and T47's text tests; T17's closure item (S15). Amended in L144 (L143, owner: "acepto", to "Cuando un repo no tiene CI, ¿el cierre exige que la entrega haya corrido los tests en el head entregado y que hayan pasado?"; L143: "Yes: with no CI, closure requires that delivery ran the tests on the delivered head and that they passed."): delivery establishes no CI with the same verb, never a second detector: before it appends its entry it runs `labdrian review ci --landing <delivered head> --head <delivered head>` (a commit holds itself: `IsAncestor` answers true for "b itself", `engine/review/app/app.go:144`) and puts the verb's exact line in the entry; when the verb exits 0, the run of the test runner at the delivered head is required: "not run" (the worktree is not at that head) or a failing result closes nothing and returns status blocked naming it; when it exits non-zero, the L123 sentence stands as written ("not run" allowed). The foreman's Close row accepts the no-CI alternative only when delivery's entry holds the test runner's command run at the delivered head with a passing result; "not run" or a failure is missing evidence, never a closure. Acceptance: T53's text tests. Amended in L147 (T49; the T8 run stopped at Open on "odd/goals/gadu-generate-opencode-message.json cannot be read (not found)" and L135 advised "workflow open needs a goal file no role writes"; the owner answered the foreman in that run: "Sí, que el shaper escriba odd/goals/gadu-generate-opencode-message.json."): the shaper writes the goal `labdrian workflow open` reads, `odd/goals/<f>.json` under the repository's root (`engine/cmd/workflow_open.go:77-90`), as a Goal of version 3 (`engine/goal/goal.go:122-126`) whose `goal_id` is the feature, when the foreman's prompt names that path; the foreman names it whenever the file does not exist, and its Shape stage advances only once the file exists; the shaper never rewrites a goal file that exists, and reports it. The goal is checked where it is read: `workflow open` refuses a missing, invalid or other-goal-id file before writing anything (`engine/workflow/app/open.go:192-202`), and the foreman relays the refusal (its Open row). Assumption: the owner's answer, given in the T8 run for one feature, holds for every feature. Assumption: the shaper's default `memory_directive` is the `odd` profile's ceiling, scope `project` and sources `engram`, `longterm-mem`, `procedural-skills` (`engine/workflowprofile/workflowprofile.go:90,99`), so a goal narrows memory only when the request asks; a memorycard read is judged as source `engram` (`engine/projection/gate.go:319-320`). Acceptance: T49's text tests and a goal built from the shaper's key list that `goal.Parse` accepts. Amended in L148: the review stop hook asks a session only for the commits of its branch that the default branch lacks and, when the branch tracks a branch of another name, that branch lacks too; with no upstream, or one of the branch's own name, it asks as before. Amended in L152 (findings on 4eb00fb9: the shaper role ships to every install while the goal file its text copies the directive from, `odd/goals/labdrian-orchestrator-opencode.json`, exists only in this repository; the foreman never names the project, and `goal.Parse` requires a non-blank `project_id`, `engine/goal/goal.go:140-145`; a narrowed directive has no schema): the engine prints the four fields of the goal it can know, and the shaper writes only the rest. A read-only verb `labdrian workflow goal-base --feature <f> [--project <id>]` (name proposed, T55) prints one JSON line, `{"version":3,"project_id":"<P>","goal_id":"<f>","memory_directive":<D>}`, where `<D>` is the memory default of the `odd` profile as `memoryscope.DefaultFor` builds it (`engine/memoryscope/memoryscope.go:159-172`; `odd` is the profile `workflow open` creates, `engine/workflow/app/open.go:27`), and `<P>` is `--project`, else the last `/`-separated part of the project's shared identity, the one `workflow open` records for the memory gate (`engine/cmd/workflow.go:303-314`, chain `identity/resolve/resolve.go:118-144`: the `.longterm-mem-project` file, the origin remote, the main checkout's directory name); `<P>` must pass `workflow.ValidateIdentifier` (`engine/workflow/workflow.go:475-487`), else the verb exits 2 naming the identity and saying to pass `--project <id>`. `workflow open` itself resolves no `project_id`: it takes the goal's (`open.go:140`). Assumption: this rule is the project-id convention; in this repository it gives `labdrian-sdd-overlay` (origin `git@github.com:labdrian-ai/labdrian-sdd-overlay.git`; the normalized form `github.com/labdrian-ai/labdrian-sdd-overlay` is unverified, the RED fixture confirms it), the `project_id` of all 12 goals under `odd/goals/`. When the goal file does not exist, the foreman runs the verb and puts its stdout in the shaper's prompt with the path; the shaper copies `version`, `project_id`, `goal_id` and `memory_directive` verbatim, writes no goal without that output (a blocker it reports), and narrows the directive only when the request asks, only by removing sources, or by setting `scope` to `goal`, or to `none` with `sources` `[]` (the forms `memoryscope` accepts: scopes none < goal < project, sources a duplicate-free subset of the closed set, `memoryscope.go:21-38,117-139`); no shaper file names a memory source token or a goal file of this repository (the L151 guard stays). The L151 instruction to copy the directive from `odd/goals/labdrian-orchestrator-opencode.json` is withdrawn. Acceptance: T55's command and text tests.
- **S4 — delivery hands its result to the foreman.** L5: "la entrega es del delivery al orquestador". The delivery role returns its result to the foreman, never to the owner directly; the foreman then runs the closure `inception-pipeline` names and reports to the owner. Deliverable: `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` and `skills/inception-pipeline/SKILL.md` name the parent `foreman` and state the delivery → foreman hand-off; `roles/delivery/body.md` changes only if it addresses the owner (to be checked by the builder). Assumption: the hand-off is the delivery agent's envelope returning to its parent, not a new `delivery → foreman` record; `engine/roles` keeps delivery terminal (`roles.go:129`; accepted, L11). Acceptance: the skills' text tests (`engine/skills/role_chain_artifact_test.go` and its kind) pass with the new wording; the T8 run shows the delivery envelope reaching the foreman.
- **S5 — Generated once for both runtimes.** L2.5: "en ambos runtimes". `engine/agentgen` emits `agents/foreman.md` (Claude Code) and `opencode/agents/foreman.md` (OpenCode primary agent) from the one source. Assumption: no Pi or Codex file for the foreman (not requested). Acceptance: `agentgen.Check` passes; a test pins the foreman's model, tools and the two paths; the eight existing roles' Claude, Pi and Codex files are byte-identical to before.
- **S6 — A Claude Code main session runs as the foreman.** A main session is not a subagent; T1 (L10) proved the mechanism: the Claude Code settings key `{"agent":"foreman"}` runs `~/.claude/agents/foreman.md` as the main session with CLAUDE.md, hooks and MCP still active, and the agent's `tools:` line names the subagent tool `Agent`. The default system prompt is replaced by an SDK prefix plus the agent body, so the body must stand alone (S2). Change to `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`: the block from `<!-- gentle-ai:orchestrator -->` to `<!-- /gentle-ai:orchestrator -->` (lines 35-429 on 2026-11-10, unchanged on 2026-11-16, L16; the markers, not the line numbers, delimit the removal) is removed, with a backup first and the owner's approval; the other gentle-ai blocks leave under S17. Acceptance: a fresh Claude Code session in this repository identifies as the foreman, launches a role, and the removed block is absent.
- **S7 — OpenCode role agents.** `engine/agentgen` emits `opencode/agents/<role>.md` for the eight roles plus the foreman in the v2 agent schema measured by T4 (L10: `<config>/agents/<id>.md` with `description`, `mode`, `model: openai/<id>#<variant>`, `permissions: [{action, resource, effect}]`, `steps`; `default_agent` in `opencode.json`), each role's Claude tool allowlist mapped to v2 permissions and its model and variant read from S18; deployed through the existing `opencode-agent` manifest route (`engine/skills/routes.go:30-31,230-237`). Acceptance: generator test table like `engine/agentgen/roles_test.go`; a `deny` on `edit` (which covers the `patch` tool) and on `shell` removes those tools (enforced, L10 T4), so reviewer and polisher get `edit` and `shell` denied and verifier `edit` denied, matching their Claude tool lists; a role with no `Bash` gets `shell` denied and one with no `Edit`/`Write` gets `edit` denied. To match the Claude tool lists for the rest of the v2 tools L10 T2 listed (L16): no role holds the subagent tool (each body says "never launch sub-agents"), so the eight roles get `subagent` denied; no role's Claude list names `WebFetch`, `WebSearch`, `Skill` or a question tool (`engine/agentgen/roles.go:66-146`; a role reads the SKILL.md paths its prompt names with `Read`), so each of the eight roles gets `question`, `skill`, `webfetch`, `websearch` and `experimental_image_generate` denied, and `execute` denied when its Claude list names no MCP tool (reviewer, `roles.go:126`). The foreman is not under the eight roles' set (L19): derived the same way from its Claude list (L7 generator row: `Edit`, `Write`, `Bash`, the subagent tool, MCP tools), it keeps `edit`, `shell`, `subagent` and `execute`; it also keeps `skill`, because S13's recorded run loads a skill body in its session; it keeps `question` too, since its Claude list holds `AskUserQuestion` (T48, L134), and gets `webfetch`, `websearch` and `experimental_image_generate` denied. Acceptance for the foreman (L21): a row of the generator test table pins `opencode/agents/foreman.md` with `mode: primary`, the frontier model and `#variant` of S18, no deny on `edit`, `shell`, `subagent`, `execute` or `skill` or `question`, and, among the built-in tools, a deny on exactly `webfetch`, `websearch` and `experimental_image_generate` (the server rules of the L103 amendment below come in addition). Assumption to verify (L16): a `deny` on those tools is enforced as it is for `edit` and `shell`; L10 measured only those two. Amended in L100, rewritten in L103 (evidence L99 B3: "the memory gate cannot see memorycard reads under Code Mode: the tool is `execute`"; answered by L102 (1): under Code Mode the inner call fires its own `execute.before` as `memorycard_mem_search` with its arguments, so every server keeps Code Mode on, S12). Under Code Mode an agent that keeps `execute` reaches every tool of every server through it: the verifier (`Read, Grep, Glob, Bash, mcp__memorycard__mem_get`, `engine/agentgen/roles.go:132-136`) holds `execute` in its generated file (`opencode/agents/verifier.md` denies no `execute`) and so every memorycard and codegraph tool (L100 named this role the reviewer; the reviewer's list, `roles.go:129`, names no MCP tool and its file denies `execute`). To keep matching the Claude tool lists, each of the eight roles and the foreman gets, for each MCP server the OpenCode `opencode.json` writes (memorycard, longterm-mem, codegraph), a deny on `<server>_*` followed by an allow on each `<server>_<tool>` its Claude list names, in that order: the rule form L102 (2) measured (the last matching rule wins; a `memorycard_*` deny removes the tools; a later allow of `memorycard_mem_get` re-enables only that one; allow-then-deny removes all). `execute` keeps its rule: denied exactly when the Claude list names no MCP tool. Assumption to verify in T17's re-run: that a `<server>_*` deny also refuses an inner call made through `execute` (L102 (2) does not record the Code Mode setting it ran under), and that the pattern holds for the hyphenated `longterm-mem_*` (only `memorycard_*` was measured). Acceptance: rows of the generator test table pin those rules for the verifier (allow `memorycard_mem_get` only), the polisher, the reviewer and the foreman, and a test fails when, in any generated file, an allow of a server precedes that server's deny. Amended in L117 (evidence L113 B5, quoted in S8): the `skill` deny of the eight roles stays; the skills a role needs reach it as absolute SKILL.md paths in its launch prompt (S8), so no role needs the `skill` tool.
- **S8 — v2 runtime-parity plugin.** Rework `engine/runtime/labdrian-runtime-parity-plugin.mjs` and the 1.x adapter, now split across `engine/runtime/opencode.go:33-170`, `opencode_plugin.go`, `opencode_contracts.go` and `opencode_records.go` (L16), into a v2 plugin that default-exports `Plugin.define({id, setup(ctx)})`, installed at `<config>/plugins/labdrian-runtime-parity/index.js` with plugin id `labdrian-runtime-parity` (the 1.x adapter's name kept, `engine/runtime/opencode_plugin.go:9-11`; its config and active-marker file names stay) (auto-loaded, no build step; L29), importing `Plugin` from `@opencode/plugin` and `Tool.Error` from `@opencode/plugin/promise/tool` (L10 T2); contract injection moves from the 1.x `task`/`subagent_type` tool to v2's `subagent` tool by mutating `input.prompt` in `execute.before`, which reaches the child session (L10 T2); the active-marker and status semantics of the adapter stay. One file, two owners in sequence (L29): T10 creates the plugin file and owns its shape, install path and records; T11 starts only after T10's two commits and adds the hook bridge (S10) to that same file. Acceptance: OpenCode v2.2.7 loads it without the L3 `PluginModule.LoadError`; existing adapter tests updated, a node-run test of the injection on `subagent`. Amended in L100 (evidence L99 B4: "the reviewer (shell denied) cannot obtain the diff: the foreman wrote it to ~/.cache/opencode (outside the sandbox, removed) and copied it into the read-only review worktree"): the reviewer must receive the diff in its prompt, as `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:34` asks ("Send `reviewer` the diff (up to 150 KB, else one launch per commit; never truncated)") and as `roles/reviewer/body.md:8` reads it ("The candidate is the diff in this prompt (base, head, diff_sha256) plus the read-only worktree path it names"). On OpenCode the plugin, not the foreman, appends it: on a `subagent` launch of agent `reviewer`, `execute.before` passes the prompt to an engine verb that appends the frozen context of the binding the prompt names, the same bytes `labdrian review launch --runtime codex` hands Codex (`engine/review/app/launch.go:134-154`: the changed paths and the -U3 diff in one fence); a prompt the verb refuses (no binding named, over the 150 KB launch limit, not the brief of its candidate) denies the launch with the verb's reason. Acceptance: Go tests of the verb, a node-run test of the reviewer launch, and `review launch --runtime codex` output byte-identical to the base. Amended in L117 (evidence L113 B5: the builder returned "status: blocked ... `test-first`, `go-testing`, `work-unit-commits`, `branch-pr`, `odd-feature-doc`, `sdd-time-estimation`, and `adhd-output` each returned `Permission denied: skill`", after a handoff that named skills as "## Skills to load before work: test-first, go-test"; L115: "0 files under ov/skills/role-chain, ov/roles, ov/opencode/agents name a SKILL.md path; roles/builder/body.md:10 "Follow the `test-first` skill""): on OpenCode every skill a role needs reaches it as an absolute SKILL.md path under the header `## Skills to load before work` (`engine/contract/contract.go:32`) of its launch prompt. (a) Skills a role body names: one shared function returns the ids a role body names in the form `` `<id>` skill`` (today `roles/builder/body.md:9-10`, `roles/delivery/body.md:8`, `roles/shaper/body.md:8`, `roles/foreman/body.md:16`); the agentgen generator fails, naming the role, the id and `skills/<id>/SKILL.md`, when a body names an id with no such file in the repository; the OpenCode adapter adds to the prompt config it writes, for each of the eight roles and each id its body names, one entry in the existing contract-entry shape (`engine/runtime/opencodeprompt/config.go:23-32`: `contract_path` the overlay's `skills/<id>/SKILL.md` made absolute as the contracts are, `engine/runtime/opencode_contracts.go:31-40`; `included_phases` the role; `injection_point` the header), which the plugin's existing injection (`mutatePrompt`/`injectPrompt`, `labdrian-runtime-parity-plugin.mjs:79-117`) adds with no plugin change. (b) Skills a launch prompt lists: on a `subagent` launch, after the gate and before the frozen context (so the diff appended to a reviewer brief is never read as a skill list), the plugin passes the prompt to an engine verb that reads the section under the first exact header line up to the next `#` heading: a bare skill id with `<skills root>/<id>/SKILL.md` on disk becomes that absolute path, an absolute path to an existing file and a blank line stay, and a line that starts with the header text but is not the header alone (L113's form), or any other line, refuses the launch with exit 2 and one stderr line saying what to write instead, which the plugin throws as `Tool.Error`, as for `review frozen-context` (`:403-425`); the skills root is the overlay's `skills` directory, written by the adapter into the plugin config. Per runtime: OpenCode gets (a) and (b); Claude Code, Codex and Pi get only (a)'s generator check (no body names a missing skill). Today, read from the code: on Claude Code no role tool list holds `Skill` (`engine/agentgen/roles.go:69-148`) and `gate-task` injects only the contracts of `engine/cmd/contracts.go:15-18`, so a Claude role learns a skill's path only from its parent's prompt (the Skill Registry Protocol of the live `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` gentle-ai block, which S6 and S17 remove; L73: the foreman's body does not say where it takes skill paths from) or by searching (this shaper, on Claude Code, was named `odd-feature-doc` without a path and found `skills/odd-feature-doc/SKILL.md` with Glob); Codex has no launch hook the engine installs (`engine/settings/codexhooks/document.go:36-37` match memory tools only) and reads skills from `~/.codex/skills` (`bin/lib/targets.sh:16`; "No test observes Codex loading a skill or an agent", `engine/capability/declarations.go:167`); Pi's role tools hold no skill tool (`roles.go:195-208`) and its skills come in the labdrian-pi package ("No test observes Pi loading a skill or an agent", `declarations.go:188`). Acceptance: the generator test; a runtime test over this repository's role bodies that every id each of the eight bodies names has its entry and its file; node-run tests: a builder launch gets the `test-first` and `odd-feature-doc` paths, a bare id is rewritten, an unknown id, the colon form and a prose line are refused with the verb's line, a reviewer brief's appended diff is left alone; Go tests of the verb; every other launch byte-identical to the base.

- **S10 — Hook bridge.** The plugin delivers to the same engine commands, with `--source opencode`, each engine event (`engine/hookwire/hookwire.go:44-58`) through the equivalents T2/T3 measured (L10): PreToolUse from `ctx.tool.hook("execute.before")` (fires for built-ins, inner MCP calls under Code Mode and child sessions; deny by throwing `Tool.Error`), PostToolUse from `execute.after`, UserPromptSubmit from `ctx.session.hook("prompt")` (it also fires in each child session), SessionStart from `ctx.session.hook("context")` with `event.system.push` (reaches main and child sessions; not `instructions.entry.put`, which reaches only its own session), Stop from `experimental.idle` with `ctx.session.synthetic` forcing another turn (an experimental API, declared as such in S14). - **S16 — foreman discipline.** L8: "que tenga una personalidad estruturada guiada ppr ser riguroso no complaciente". Specified as behaviors, not adjectives; tone and language stay with the owner's output style (Claude Code) or AGENTS.md (OpenCode), and the role sets discipline only. Each behavior has a check a reviewer can make from a session transcript:
  - B1 Verifies before agreeing: when the owner or a role states a fact about code or state, the reply says it will check, a tool call checks it, and only then the reply agrees or disagrees. Check: no agreement precedes a checking tool call in that turn.
  - B2 Disagrees with evidence: a wrong claim is contradicted with path:line or command output. Check: a seeded wrong claim in the T8 run is contradicted with a citation.
  - B3 Reports only observed checks: every check a reply calls passing has a command in the transcript with that result; anything else is "not run". Check: match each "pass" in replies to a command and its output.
  - B4 Treats role reports as records: before advancing on a role's report, the foreman re-runs at least one command it reported; a "fixed" verdict on a review finding goes to the verifier. Check: a re-run follows each role return that the foreman advances on.
  - B5 Advances a stage only with its evidence (L9 stage table). Check: no `workflow open` before a `shaped: L<n>` mark exists; no second builder launch for a new commit before the previous commit's review result or exemption is stored; no delivery launch before a clear review record.
  - B6 Names the one decision and stops: when blocked on the owner, the reply asks exactly one question with a recommendation and tradeoff and makes no tool call after it. Check: exactly one question, and no tool call after it in the turn; the question may be reply text or one `AskUserQuestion` call (OpenCode: `question`), which is then the turn's last tool call (T48, L137).
  - B7 Fixed reply shape: every reply that reports work ends with the lines Decision, Outcome, Evidence, Next. Check: present in each such reply.
  - B8 Writes only the feature document: Check: no Write or Edit tool call on a path outside `odd/tasks/` and the session scratchpad (temporary stdin files for engine verbs such as `roles append` and `review record`, never inside the repository; L136), and the memorycard mirror save. Enforcement: instructions only, on both runtimes (L11 accepted L9 risk 7: a path guard only after a real failure); the foreman holds `Edit`/`Write` in Claude Code (L7 generator row) and `edit` in OpenCode (S7), so this transcript check is the only control, and S14 declares B8 prose-only.
  - B9 Uses only labdrian roles: Check: every subagent launch names builder, shaper, prototyper, sweeper, polisher, reviewer, verifier or delivery.
  - B10 Leaves owner actions to the owner: Check: no `git push`, `gh pr create`, `gh pr merge`, `labdrian skills approve` or `shaper clearance record` without an owner message asking for it in the transcript; a merge is preceded by `gh pr checks` with no failing line.
- **S17 — Only labdrian's own content remains (gentle-ai detach).** L11: "tenemos que mejorar todo.lo que aprendemos.de gentle y dejar solo lo nusdtro adaptado y mejorado para nuestro flujo". Every gentle-ai block leaves `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and, on the owner's "sacalo" (L71), `~/AGENTS.md`, `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` and `~/.codex/agents.md`, where the same owner-rules source T26 deploys takes their place (T31, L76), and every gentle-provided asset the owner's flow relies on is either adopted (adapted, improved, owned by labdrian, with its home in this repository and deployed by `labdrian apply`) or dropped, with the reason. Persona and voice are in scope: they get a labdrian-owned home, and the owner's tone and language rules keep their substance. Owner decisions on single assets: the ten `sdd-*` skills are dropped (L68: "y borralas"); `branch-pr` and `issue-creation` are adopted by a rewrite for the labdrian flow (L74: "podemos rescribirla y modernizarla parw nuestro flujo"; L75: "dale"; T32, T33); `systemic-issue-triage` is rewritten in a later feature after the Phase 11 launch (L75), so in this feature it is neither adopted nor dropped: it stays in the repository and under `~/.claude/skills` as it is (its `managed` manifest row, its `core` registry entry and its author unchanged), the inventory records it as deferred to that feature, and no task here retags, rewrites or deletes it. Deliverables: (a) an inventory, one row per asset: where it is (path:line), what labdrian learned from it, adopt or drop, the reason, and for an adopted asset its new home and the verb that deploys it; (b) the adoption units the inventory names; (c) one owner-approved, backed-up removal from the live files, after which no remaining hook or entry invokes the `gentle-ai` binary; no `gentle-ai sync` re-add check is run, because L13: "no vamos a correr mas gentle-ai sync". In `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` the markers delimit every removal; `~/.claude/settings.json` has no markers, so each of its entries is identified by its content: a hook entry whose command runs the `gentle-ai` binary, the `outputStyle` and `theme` keys by their values, and the plugin source `Gentleman-Programming/engram`. Every line number below is evidence as read on 2026-11-10 and confirmed on 2026-11-16 (L16), never a removal range, and may drift. Preliminary inventory, read for L12 and to be confirmed and completed by T21: `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` markers `gentle-ai:persona` (1-33), `gentle-ai:orchestrator` (35-429, with `authority-first-terminal-procedure` 250-262), `gentle-ai:codegraph-guidance` (431-460), `gentle-ai:agent-routing` (462-566, with `gentle-ai:remote-authorization` 556-565); `user:memorycard-store` (568-592) is the owner's and stays; `~/.claude/settings.json` `outputStyle: "Gentleman"` (496), theme `custom:gentleman` (525), hooks `gentle-ai review stop-hook --agent claude-code` (310, 348), `gentle-ai telemetry runtime claude --json` (358, 388), UserPromptSubmit `gentle-ai skill-registry refresh` (411), plugin source `Gentleman-Programming/engram` (486); `~/.claude/output-styles/gentleman.md`; skills under `~/.claude/skills` whose frontmatter says `author: gentleman-programming` (the `sdd-*` family, `_shared`, `branch-pr`, `chained-pr`, `work-unit-commits`, `judgment-day`, `comment-writer`, `issue-creation`, `skill-creator`, `skill-improver`, `skill-registry`, `cognitive-doc-design`, `go-testing`, `rdd-defect-workflow`, `inception-pipeline`, `project-inception`, `project-manifest`, `project-architect`, `roadmap-maker`, `requirements-from-transcripts`, `kadia-*`). Not gentle-ai's despite the name: the `gentle-ai-overlay` hook commands in `settings.json` are labdrian's own engine under its old binary name (renamed at Phase 11, MEMORY.md index "Rename to wizzarpot"), so they stay. Acceptance: after the removal, `rg 'gentle-ai:' ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` finds nothing, `outputStyle` names the labdrian-owned output style T21 adopts (by its file under `~/.claude/output-styles/` that `labdrian apply` deploys), `theme` is absent (Claude Code's default theme; L69: "volvamos al grma ñor defecto"), no plugin source anywhere in `settings.json` names `Gentleman-Programming/engram` (found by content, not by line), and nothing in either file runs the `gentle-ai` binary; `rg -n 'gentle-ai:' ~/AGENTS.md ~/.codex/AGENTS.md ~/.codex/agents.md` finds nothing and each of those three files holds one `<!-- labdrian:owner-rules -->` block (L71). Check: run `rg -n '(^|[^\w.-])gentle-ai([^\w/-]|$)' ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (Markdown, no escapes), and over `~/.claude/settings.json` decode every string value before matching, because in raw JSON an escape such as `\n`, `\t` or a `\uXXXX` code (the code of `&` ends in the digit `6`) puts a word character (`n`, `t`, `6`) just before a call on a later line and the pattern misses it: `jq -r '(paths | .[] | strings), (.. | strings)' ~/.claude/settings.json | rg -n -e '(^|[^\w.-])gentle-ai([^\w/-]|$)' -e 'Gentleman-Programming/engram'` (the second pattern finds the plugin source, which never contains the word `gentle-ai`). Every object key and every string value is searched decoded and line by line (hooks, `statusLine`, `args` arrays, `enabledPlugins` keys such as `engram@Gentleman-Programming/engram`, and every other value); the line numbers are of the decoded output, so each match is found in the file by its text. Read each match: a `gentle-ai` match counts only when that value runs the binary; a `Gentleman-Programming/engram` match always counts. The pattern matches a call wrapped or not (bare, `sh -c 'gentle-ai x'`, `cd x && gentle-ai y`, `timeout 5 /usr/local/bin/gentle-ai z`, `echo|gentle-ai`) and never `gentle-ai-overlay` (labdrian's own engine, which stays) or a `gentle-ai` directory (`~/.gentle-ai/y`, `/opt/gentle-ai/bin`). The inventory accounts for every row above. Delivery: one feature, L14: "dejalo todo en una sola feature"; T21 writes the adoption units as tasks in this document.

The foreman takes the frontier row (it is the parent, `models.md:3`). `engine/agentgen` reads the model ids from that table instead of hard-coding them (`engine/agentgen/roles.go:53-57`).    
    ```
    You are the foreman of the labdrian workflow: you talk with the owner, explore read-only, and launch the role agents that do the work; never do a role's work yourself.
    Read every artifact the prompt locates (file path, or memorycard topic via mem_search, then mem_get) before acting. A missing required locator is a blocker: report it, never substitute another store.
    Report only commands you ran and their exact results; an unrun check is "not run", never a pass.
    Artifacts are in English.

    Change nothing the owner did not ask to change; investigation, review and planning stay read-only.
    Ask the owner at most one question, only for a decision only the owner can make, then stop and wait.
    Start each unit of work with the `inception-pipeline` skill and run every hop with the `role-chain` skill; move the workflow only with the `labdrian workflow` verbs they name.
    Write only the feature document `odd/tasks/<feature>.md` and its memorycard mirror `odd/<feature>/tasks`, following the `odd-feature-doc` skill; never write source or tests.
    Hand off by reference (the document, the task IDs, their S#), pass exact SKILL.md paths under `## Skills to load before work`, and ask each role to close with a `## Key Learnings` block.
    Carry an authorized outcome through its steps without asking to approve each; stop only for an owner decision, a denied action or missing proof.
    When delivery returns, run the closure `inception-pipeline` names, then report the verified outcome, every failed or unrun check, and the next step; push, pull requests and merges are the owner's decision.
    ```
    The second line is `agentgen`'s `memoryLine` (`engine/agentgen/roles.go:17`), kept because the foreman holds `mem_search`.

Files: Claude and OpenCode only (S5 assumption).

- **L9** (2026-11-10, shaper): design delta for L8; reworks S2, S3, adds S16, reworks T6 and T8, adds T18. T1-T5 and every other task keep their L7 design.
  - Small changes: L7 risk 6 is closed by L8. The foreman never edits source; a small change is inception-pipeline Tier 3 (`skills/inception-pipeline/references/tier-selection.md:48-54`) into the role chain.
  - Steelman of the stronger body: a per-stage table of role, gate, evidence and stop point turns the owner's standing rules (memory files read for this entry) into checks the foreman runs every time, and S16 makes "riguroso no complaciente" verifiable from a transcript instead of an adjective. Strongest counter-argument: about 45 lines restate the stage order that `role-chain` and `inception-pipeline` already own, so the table can drift when a skill or verb changes. Recommendation: keep the table but limit it to what the foreman checks (the skills keep the how), and pin it with T6's test that every named verb and skill exists. Tradeoff accepted: some duplication of the stage order, in exchange for an explicit gate per stage.
  - Draft of `roles/foreman/body.md` (replaces the L7 draft; T6 writes it as is):
    ```
    You are the foreman of the labdrian workflow: you talk with the owner, explore read-only, and launch the labdrian role agents that do the work. You never write source, tests or another role's artifact.
    Read every artifact the prompt locates (file path, or memorycard topic via mem_search, then mem_get) before acting. A missing required locator is a blocker: report it, never substitute another store.
    Report only commands you ran and their exact results; an unrun check is "not run", never a pass.
    Artifacts are in English. Tone and language come from the owner's rules (output style or AGENTS.md); this file sets discipline, not voice.

    ## Discipline
    - Verify before you agree: when the owner or a role states a fact about code or state, say you will check it, check it, then agree or disagree citing path:line or the command output.
    - Disagree when the evidence says so; never soften a finding or a failed check to please.
    - A role's report is a record, not a fact: before you advance on it, re-run one command it reports; send every "fixed" verdict on a review finding to the verifier to refute.
    - Never advance a stage without the evidence its row in Stages names; name the evidence that is missing.
    - When only the owner can decide, name that one decision, your recommendation and its tradeoff, ask it as one question, and stop.
    - Carry an authorized outcome through its stages without asking to approve each; stop only for an owner decision, a denied action or missing evidence.
    - End every reply that reports work with four lines: Decision, Outcome, Evidence, Next.

    ## Stages
    Start each unit of work with the `inception-pipeline` skill and run every hop with the `role-chain` skill. The table says what you check; the skills say how.

    | Stage | Launch or run | Gate it meets | Advance only with | Stop for the owner when |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | Authorize | nothing: read the request | — | a request that asks for a change; otherwise stay read-only | the intent is ambiguous |
    | Tier | `inception-pipeline` tier selection; a small change is Tier 3, never an edit of yours | — | the tier and the rule that fired, recorded | rule 4 fires and one question remains |
    | Explore | codegraph, Read, Grep; the prototyper for a premise | memory gate: every memory read names the project | path:line evidence; the prototyper's resolved or open per question | — |
    | Shape | the shaper | — | every task line carries `shaped: L<n>` with its shaper Log entry | the shaper returns a fork only the owner settles |
    | Open | `labdrian workflow open --feature F --task T` | builder guard (binding) | stdout with `"bound":true` | it refuses: relay its message |
    | Build | the builder, its prompt holding `Spec: odd/tasks/<f>.md, T<n>, S<n>` and the test runner | builder guard; gate-task adds the contracts and the memory block | a commit with a `Risk:` trailer, the `test-first` table, focused test and suite results, one command re-run by you | the builder reports a product decision |
    | Sweep, polish | `labdrian review sweep`; the sweeper when it fired; the polisher when `Risk:` names an item | review stop-hook | the records `labdrian review check` asks of the commit | — |
    | Review | `labdrian review bind --base <last reviewed head> --head <commit>`, the reviewer, `review record`, the verifier on a severe finding, `review verify` | review stop-hook | a clear result; warnings and defects fixed; suggestions carried as `role-chain` says | a finding needs a product decision, or the correction round is spent |
    | Deliver | the delivery role; it runs `review check --onto` | — | delivery `success` naming the head and the results | always: push, pull request and merge are the owner's |
    | Close | closure-feedback of `inception-pipeline`, `labdrian workflow close`, the `procedural-candidates` sweep | — | the merge commit, and CI read green | — |

    ## Never
    - Never launch a general-purpose agent to write or verify; only builder, shaper, prototyper, sweeper, polisher, reviewer, verifier and delivery.
    - Never let the builder design: a task with no shaper design goes back to the prototyper (premise open) or the shaper.
    - Never put a second work unit in flight while the previous commit's review is not recorded.
    - Never run `labdrian skills approve`, record a shaper clearance, or run `gentle-ai review`: approval and clearance are the owner's, and our review is the role chain's.
    - Never merge, or arm anything that merges, before reading `gh pr checks` with no failing check and the review recorded clear.
    - While the projected workflow is paused, change nothing; resume it only on the owner's word.
    ```
    Line 2 stays `agentgen`'s `memoryLine` (`engine/agentgen/roles.go:17`).
  - Evidence per element (read for this entry; memory files under `~/.claude/projects/-home-labdrian-labdrian-sdd-overlay/memory/`):
    - Authorize: ODD protocol step 1 in the `gentle-ai:agent-routing` block (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md:462-566`).
    - Tier: `skills/inception-pipeline/SKILL.md:23-37`; `references/tier-selection.md:48-54`.
    - Explore, memory gate: `engine/cmd/usage.go:367-373` (a memorycard `mem_search` or `mem_context` must name the project); prototyper trigger `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:24`.
    - Shape: builder guard requires `shaped: L<n>` and a shaper Log entry (`usage.go:285-287`); `skills/odd-feature-doc/SKILL.md:23`; memory `design-is-shaper-work.md:11-15`.
    - Open: `usage.go:351-360`; the builder guard denies a builder outside the bound running workflow (`usage.go:290-293`); `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:20`.
    - Build: the `Spec:` line (`usage.go:285-286`); gate-task appends the memory block while a workflow is bound (`usage.go:16-19`); runner named in every builder prompt (`skills/inception-pipeline/SKILL.md:86`); `Risk:` trailer and test-first table (`roles/builder/body.md:10,12`; `skills/test-first/SKILL.md:16-27`).
    - Sweep, polish: `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:25`; commit prerequisites (`usage.go:191`); `review sweep` and `review record --role` (`usage.go:215-230`); the stop hook asks for what a commit lacks (`usage.go:264-274`).
    - Review: `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:34-38`; `usage.go:158-181` (bind, record, verify; one correction round, `usage.go:180`); memory `branch-per-work-unit.md:11-24` (per-commit chained review); `resolve-every-review-finding.md:11-17` (warnings and defects fixed, suggestions carried).
    - Deliver: `roles/delivery/body.md:9`; `usage.go:182-200`; L5 (hand-off to the foreman).
    - Close: `skills/inception-pipeline/SKILL.md:98-107` (fire after the merge only); `usage.go:322` (`workflow close`); `skills/procedural-candidates/SKILL.md:17` (T2 sweep at session close).
    - Discipline: the owner's Rules "Never agree with user claims without verification…" (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md:2-15`); memory `verifier-claims-are-records-too.md:20-26`; verifier reproduction `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:37`; core prefix "Report only commands you ran" (`roles/shaper/body.md:4`).
    - Never: memory `use-our-own-agents.md:11-15`; `design-is-shaper-work.md:11-15`; `one-slice-at-a-time.md:15`; skills approval is a human step (`usage.go:137-142`); shaper clearance (`usage.go:298-302`); gentle review off since 2026-11-05 (MEMORY.md index line "Own RDD replaces gentle review"); `merge-only-after-green-ci.md:11-17`; `no-automerge-while-review-pending.md:15`; Write and Edit denied while paused (`usage.go:367`).

- **L10** (2026-11-10, prototyper spikes T1-T5, all **continue**; parent spot-checked `SPIKE-DENIED` in the spike plugin and `FOREMAN-SPIKE` in the T1 run logs, then removed the credential-bearing scratch `/var/tmp/spike-pkmItI`; live `opencode auth list` still shows OpenAI OAuth stored). Isolation slip: `opencode auth list`/`auth export` once hit the live background service (reads only; no refresh observed).
  - T1: Claude Code settings key `{"agent":"foreman"}` runs `~/.claude/agents/foreman.md` as the main session; CLAUDE.md, hooks and MCP stay active; `tools:` names the subagent tool `Agent` (init event reports `Task`); the default system prompt is replaced by an SDK prefix + the agent body, so the body must be self-sufficient. Not measured: `--agent` vs setting precedence, skills inside a main-thread agent.
  - T2: v2 auto-loads `<config>/plugins/<name>/index.js` (`import { Plugin } from "@opencode/plugin"`; `Tool.Error` from `@opencode/plugin/promise/tool`; no build step). `execute.before` fires for built-ins (`shell`), for inner MCP calls under Code Mode (and the outer `execute`) and with `codemode:false`, and inside `subagent` child sessions; throwing `Tool.Error` denies (no `execute.after` then); mutating `input.prompt` on `subagent` injects into the child. GPT-visible tools: `glob, grep, patch, question, read, shell, skill, subagent, webfetch, websearch, execute, experimental_image_generate` — edit tool is `patch`. Name map: `shell`→Bash, `patch`→Edit, `<server>_<tool>`→`mcp__<server>__<tool>`; ignore the `execute` wrapper.
  - T3: start context via `ctx.session.hook("context")` + `event.system.push` reaches main and child sessions; `instructions.entry.put` reaches only its own session (children fire their own `prompt` hook). Stop equivalent: `experimental.idle` + `ctx.session.synthetic` forces another turn (experimental). Subagent final text is in `execute.after` for tool `subagent` (`output`).
  - T4: agents `<config>/agents/<id>.md` with `description, mode, model: openai/<id>#<variant>, permissions: [{action,resource,effect}], steps`; `default_agent` in opencode.json; `deny` on `edit`/`shell` removes the tools (enforced, `edit` covers `patch`). MCP v2 shape `mcp.servers.<n>{type:"local", command:[...], codemode}` accepted. Models: `gpt-5.6-{luna,sol,terra,cyber}`, `gpt-6-{astra,luna,sol}`, `gpt-6.1-{sol,terra}` (+`-fast`, some `-ultrafast`); variants `none|low|medium|high|xhigh|max` (astra and gpt-6.1-sol start at `low`). Not measured: resource-pattern granularity (e.g. shell allowlists).
  - T5: OpenCode reads `<config>/AGENTS.md`, not `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`. Skill precedence: config copy, then `~/.agents/skills`, then `~/.claude/skills` — a copy in `~/.config/opencode/skills` shadows the Claude one.
  - Effect on L9 risks: risk 2 (Code Mode) can stay on — inner MCP calls are hooked; risk 3 resolves toward reading `~/.claude/skills` with no second copy; risk 5 (`review launch --runtime opencode`) is unnecessary because permission deny is enforced.

  - Verb contract (T25a). Plugs into `internal/cli/cli.go`: the usage text (`:27-84`), the command list that opens the database (`:114`), the dispatch (`:128-147`), a new `a.list` beside `a.search` (`:227-256`); the query goes in `internal/memory` beside `Service.Get` (`ops.go:56`), which it must not copy, because `Get` writes `use_count` and `last_used_at` (`ops.go:57-59`).
    - `memorycard list --topic-prefix P [--topic-prefix P2 ...] [--project NAME] [--json]`. At least one non-empty `--topic-prefix`, else exit 2 with `usage: memorycard list ...` on stderr; `--project` defaults to the resolved project (`cli.go:158-164`) and an empty value is a usage error (exit 2); exit codes as `Run` states (`cli.go:93-94`).
    - Selection: memories of that project whose `topic_key` starts with any given prefix, compared literally (`substr(topic_key, 1, length(?)) = ?`, never `LIKE`, since keys hold `_`); only `status = 'active'`; rows with no topic key never match; order by `topic_key`, then `id`. No write of any kind.
    - Stored rows as they are: a split memory appears as its parts (part 1 under the key, parts 2..N under `<key>/part-i`, `internal/memory/save.go:205-224`), not joined.
    - `--json`: one object `{"project": "<p>", "memories": [{"id": <n>, "topic_key": "...", "title": "...", "body": "..."}]}`, `memories` always an array (`[]` when none). Without `--json`: one line `#<id> <topic_key>: <title>` per memory, `no results` when none (as `render.Search`, `internal/render/render.go:25-27`).
    - Fields: `topic_key` and `body` are what the consumer reads; `id` names a row in an error and is the number of the `memorycard:<id>` scheme (L35); `title` is the text form's line. Dropped: `revision` (nothing reads it; a draft's revision is its body field, and it costs a count over `memory_versions` per row, `internal/store/schema.go:31-38`) and `updated_at` (no reader). Add either when a reader exists.
    - Tests (T25a): memory level: literal match of a prefix holding `_` and `%`, two prefixes in one call, another project's rows excluded, superseded and archived excluded, `use_count` unchanged after a list, a split memory listed as its parts; CLI level, in the style of `internal/cli/cli_test.go:72-125`: the JSON shape with `[]` for none, the text form, exit 2 with no prefix, with an empty prefix and with `--project ""`, and `help` naming `list`. README: a `list` entry under "What the commands return" (`README.md:83`).

The port stays `skills.CandidateSource`; `engine/skills/memorycardlist.Source{Executable, Runner}` stands behind it with the outbound process port `CommandRunner` of the same shape as the Engram adapter's (`engramexport/source.go:16-20`), `execrunner.New()` in production (`engine/cmd/skills_deps.go:35`) and a fake runner in tests. It passes `--project` explicitly (the engine's resolver, `skills_deps.go:39-49`, never memorycard's own) and the three prefixes `procedural/candidates/`, `procedural/drafts/` and `goal:`; it keeps a row only when a domain function exported from `engine/skills`, built on `splitGoalPrefix` (`candidate_record.go:53-63`, the existing rule, reused rather than re-implemented as `engramexport`'s `topicFamily` did at `source.go:112-128`), puts it in one of the two families. It refuses, as an error that `skills candidates` already turns into exit 2 (`skills_candidates_cli.go:68-71`): a runner failure (stderr relayed, so an older memorycard's `unknown command "list"` reaches the owner), a missing or null `memories` array (as `engramexport/source.go:90-110`), and a row whose key ends in `/part-<n>` (a split candidate record cannot be parsed). Tests: argument list pinned, both families, a goal-scoped key kept, an unrelated `goal:` key dropped, each refusal. Deliberate difference from `topicFamily`: `goal:/procedural/candidates/x` (empty goal id) is no longer a family member, because `splitGoalPrefix` refuses an empty id; one test pins it. Commit (b) deletes `engramexport` (231 lines, `source.go` 128 + `source_test.go` 103) and the Engram wording of `candidate_source.go:3,9,15`, `skills_candidates_cli.go:15` and `usage.go:129`; the only importer is `skills_deps.go:14,35`.
  - Steelman: a verb in memorycard keeps labdrian behind memorycard's public interface, exactly as `CandidateSource` keeps the domain behind a port, so a schema v5 breaks a memorycard test, not `skills candidates` in silence (L19 risk 1); the contract is the smallest that serves the one consumer, and T25b swaps one adapter in one composition line. Strongest counter-argument: the work now spans two repositories and a binary install before T8, and the `goal:` prefix makes every goal-scoped memory of the project cross the process boundary with its body, to be filtered in the engine; a SQLite read (A) would need neither. Recommendation: T25a then T25b as shaped. Tradeoff accepted: one more repository, merge and install on the path to T8, and a wider `goal:` read, in exchange for no coupling to memorycard's private schema. Alternative not chosen: a second engine port (a generic topic lister) under `CandidateSource`; one consumer does not pay for it.
  - Order: T25a and T23 can run beside each other (different repositories); T25b after T25a's commit; T8 after T25a is merged and installed and T25b is committed.
  - Risks:
    1. Install (owner/environment): T8's live run needs a `memorycard` on PATH with `list`; the build and install route of `/home/labdrian/.local/bin/memorycard` was not read. The parent confirms it before T8.
    2. Project names, not verified: the engine resolves a project from `--project-id`, `.longterm-mem-project`, the origin remote, then the main checkout root (`skills_deps.go:39-49`); memorycard from `MEMORYCARD_PROJECT`, a `.memorycard` file, the git remote name, the git root name (`cli.go:76-78`). If they differ for a repository, records the skill saves through the MCP tool are listed under another name and `skills candidates` shows none. T8's run observes it.
    3. Open product decision, not decided here: legacy `engram:<id>` and new `memorycard:<id>` occurrence ids are opaque strings to the engine (`candidate_record.go:148-155`), so one observation recorded under both counts twice towards a threshold. Mapping them needs the `import_map` lookup (L35) through another memorycard verb; T25 does not do it. Whether that mapping is wanted, and where, is the owner's call.
    4. Volume, unmeasured: how many active `goal:` memories a project holds; the third prefix may move far more bytes than the two families. A narrower verb is a later change if it shows.

- **L46** (2026-11-16, shaper): design of the legacy-id translation on the owner's answer, closing L37 risk 3. Rewrites S3 (one clause), T23, T25b (package name only; the rest of its design stays as L37, L42 and L43 give it), adds T25c and T25d, and updates T8's blocker clause; every other S# and T# keeps its earlier design.
  - Owner, verbatim, to the parent's question "¿agregamos esa traducción para que no cuente doble, o aceptamos el doble conteo de las observaciones viejas?": "traducelos".
  - Method: read-only. memorycard read at `/home/labdrian/memorycard-worktrees/list-topic-prefix` (branch `feat/list-topic-prefix`, not merged); it has no `.codegraph/` index, so files were read directly. No command was run; no test was run.
  - Evidence, memorycard. `import_map(source, source_key, part default 1, memory_id references memories(id) on delete cascade, primary key (source, source_key, part))` (`internal/store/schema.go:74-81`). The Engram importer writes `source = 'engram'` and `source_key` = the Engram observation id as decimal text (`internal/importer/engram.go:21,104`), only for observations with `deleted_at is null` (`:69-71`), one row per stored part with `part = i+1` (`internal/memory/import.go:123-138`); a record redacted to nothing gets no row (`:113-115`); a record whose content already exists in the project maps to that existing memory (`import.go:237-247`, counted as a duplicate at `:139-144`), so two Engram ids can map to one memory. The importer itself resolves a key by its part-1 row (`import.go:270`); `deleted.go:101-102` reads all parts. The CLI has `list` on this branch (`internal/cli/cli.go:43-52,125,150,277-311`), every command but `help`, `version`, `project` and `session` opens the database through `a.open()` (`:125-136`), and nothing exposes `import_map` (searched: only `internal/accept`, `cmd/mc-accept`, `internal/memory` and `internal/store` read it).
  - Evidence, engine. The engine never decides a promotion: the agent derives `OccurrenceCount` (`skills/_shared/procedural-candidate-detection.md:112-113`) and emits when it first reaches `Threshold` (`:163`, `:170`); the engine parses `Occurrences` as opaque strings and reports an anomaly when the stated count differs from the distinct ids (`engine/skills/candidate_record.go:147-155`), and `skills candidates` prints the stated count (`engine/cmd/skills_candidates_cli.go:138,156-157`). So the double count is born in the agent's derivation and made visible, or hidden, by the engine's check: both sides need the translation. Item 4 of the contract (`procedural-candidate-detection.md:114-116`: every listed id must resolve with a get) also needs it, because memorycard's `mem_get` takes a memorycard id.
  - Design, memorycard (T25c). A separate verb on its own branch, not an extension of `feat/list-topic-prefix`: that branch is a reviewed unit with its own feature document and five commits (L42), and resolving import keys is a different query on a different table; the price is a small, certain rebase conflict in `cli.go`'s usage text and command list (`:27-95,125`), avoided by branching after T25a's merge. Contract:
    - `memorycard resolve --source S --key K [--key K2 ...] [--json]`. `--source` required and non-empty; at least one `--key`, each non-empty; no positional argument; otherwise exit 2 with `usage: memorycard resolve --source S --key K [--key K2 ...] [--json]` on stderr; exit codes as `Run` states (`cli.go:104-105`).
    - Per key: `select memory_id from import_map where source = ? and source_key = ? and part = 1` (the importer's own lookup, `import.go:270`; equivalent to L35's `order by part limit 1`, since an import always writes part 1 first, `import.go:123-138`). Read-only: no row of any table is written, no `use_count` touched. Project-free: `source_key` is the source's own id, so `--project` is not taken.
    - Unknown key (never imported, deleted in Engram before the import, redacted to nothing, or a key that is not an Engram id): reported, not an error; the verb exits 0. `--json`: `{"source": "<S>", "ids": [{"key": "<K>", "memory_id": <n>|null}]}`, one entry per `--key` in the order given, duplicates echoed, `null` for unknown. Text: one line per key, `<S>:<K> #<n>` or `<S>:<K> unknown`.
    - Tests: memory level: a mapped key, an unknown key, a split record resolving to its part-1 memory, two Engram keys merged into one memory resolving to the same id, another source's key not matched, no write (row counts and `use_count` unchanged); CLI level: the JSON shape with `null`, the text form, input order and duplicates kept, exit 2 for no `--key`, an empty `--key`, no or empty `--source` and a positional argument, and `help` naming `resolve`.
  - Design, engine (T25d), hexagonal: the port `skills.LegacyOccurrenceResolver` in the domain package, its CLI adapter `memorycardcli.Resolver` behind it (the same package as T25b's `Source`, renamed from `memorycardlist` so one package holds the adapters of one outside tool), a fake runner in adapter tests and a fake resolver in use-case tests, wired only in the composition root (`engine/cmd/skills_deps.go:23-37`). Translation happens in the domain, not in the adapter: the adapter returns a `legacy number → memory id` map and never edits record text; the domain function maps `engram:<digits>` to `memorycard:<m>` and the distinct check (`candidate_record.go:147-155`) counts translated ids. `app.ListCandidates` (`list_candidates.go:28-50`) parses, collects the distinct legacy numbers of all candidate records, calls the resolver once, and computes the check with the map; with no legacy id it never calls the resolver, so a project with no Engram history needs no `resolve` verb. Effects the owner sees: a record listing `engram:<n>` and `memorycard:<m>` for one observation with `OccurrenceCount: 2` now shows the anomaly "OccurrenceCount is 2 but 1 distinct occurrences are listed"; output for a record with no legacy id is unchanged; the stated count column is unchanged. An unknown legacy id stays `engram:<n>` and counts as one distinct id, with no new anomaly (it may be an Engram observation deleted before the import, `engram.go:69-71`).
  - Skill text (T23): new ids are `memorycard:<id>`; a legacy `engram:<n>` is never rewritten in a stored record; before appending `memorycard:<m>` to a record that lists legacy ids, and when deriving `OccurrenceCount` or `OccurrencesSincePromotion`, the agent resolves them with `memorycard resolve --source engram --key <n> ... --json` and treats `engram:<n>` → `<m>` as the same occurrence as `memorycard:<m>`; reading a legacy occurrence is resolve, then `mem_get`; an unknown id stays and counts as itself.
  - Order: T25c beside T25b and T23's early reading (different repositories); T23 after T25c's commit; T25d after T25b (a) and T25c; T8 after T25a and T25c are merged and installed and T25b and T25d are committed.
  - Estimates (`sdd-time-estimation`, shaper row; agent-compute minutes only, no calendar or checkpoint figure): T25c 170-320 lines, minutes 30-100; T25d 220-380 lines, minutes 40-120; T23 widened from 100-260 to 130-320 lines, minutes 30-90. Basis `lines-ref n=7` for lines (this repository's class: features landed 0.7-4.2x their forecast; units under 200 lines 0.85-1.22x their high bound, over when test-heavy, as both new units are), `no-actuals n=0` for minutes; confidence low throughout. Each unit stays under one slice and has at most two pieces (T25c: query+render, CLI+docs; T25d: domain+use case, adapter+wiring).
  - Steelman: the threshold is the whole meaning of a candidate (`procedural-candidate-detection.md:163,170`); a promotion on two real occurrences of a threshold-3 candidate is a silent wrong result, and memorycard's own `import_map` is the only authority on which memory an Engram id became, so asking memorycard through its public CLI (L35's B principle) is the one correct source, and a port keeps the engine free of memorycard's schema. Strongest counter-argument: the double count needs one observation listed under both schemes, and the T1 anchor always lists the id of a save just made (`procedural-candidate-detection.md:202`), so only a re-anchoring of an already-imported observation by its new id produces it; the design adds a second verb, a second port, a process call and an install step to T8's path for a case that may never occur, and it binds counting to the import's content merge (two Engram ids merged into one memory now count once). Recommendation: T25c, T25d and the T23 text as shaped, translation at read time only. Tradeoff accepted: one more memorycard verb and merge before T8 and a resolve call wherever legacy ids are listed, forever, in exchange for no rewrite of stored records. Alternatives not chosen: an extension of `feat/list-topic-prefix` (mixes a reviewed unit); `mem_get` accepting `engram:<n>` (changes an existing verb's contract others consume); an MCP tool for the agent (a second surface; the main session and the foreman have a shell, S7, L10); the adapter rewriting record bodies (puts the record grammar in an adapter).
  - Risks:
    1. Open product decision, not decided here: two Engram observations with identical title and body in one project were merged by the import into one memory (`import.go:237-247`), so after translation they count once. Recommendation: accept it, since the import already judged them one memory; the alternative is translating only keys whose memory has a single `import_map` row, which T25c's JSON cannot tell without one more field.
    2. Open product decision, not decided here: legacy ids stay in records forever (read-time translation); rewriting each resolvable `engram:<n>` to `memorycard:<m>` the next time the agent saves a record would end the resolve calls but changes stored records (Task Size item 1). Not designed; the owner may ask for it.
    3. The agent runs `memorycard resolve` through its shell; a session without a shell tool cannot apply T23's rule. Claude Code's main session and the OpenCode foreman keep a shell (S7); the eight roles do not run this skill (not verified for every role body).
    4. Install (environment): T8 needs the installed `memorycard` rebuilt from a `main` that holds both `list` and `resolve` (L42: the installed binary is built from main `89178c7`); the parent confirms it before T8, as L37 risk 1 says for `list`.
    5. `import_map` is keyed only by `(source, source_key, part)`: a second Engram database imported under `source = 'engram'` would be skipped as already imported (`import.go:97-104`), so its ids would resolve to the first database's memories. Not seen; one Engram store exists (CLAUDE.md memorycard section).

  - Proposed texts (L14: "T21 proposes exact text for review"; T26 and T27 write them as is once the owner approves, risk 1):
    - `instructions/owner-rules.md`:
      ```
      ## Rules

      <CLAUDE.md:4-14, the eleven Rules bullets, verbatim>

      ## Expertise

      <CLAUDE.md:18 verbatim>

      ## Skills

      When a request matches an available skill, load that skill before replying. Several can apply at once; match by the files involved and by what is asked.

      ## Voice

      Tone, reply language and teaching style come from the labdrian output style; this file sets rules, not voice.

      ## CodeGraph

      - For structural or codebase questions (repository map, architecture, call flow, references, impact), use CodeGraph (`codegraph_explore`, or the read-only `codegraph` CLI commands) before broad Read, Glob or Grep.
      - In a real project with no `.codegraph/`, run `codegraph init` in the project root once, without asking; never in `$HOME`, a temporary directory or a non-project folder. Fall back to file tools only after init or use fails, and say so.
      - Put worktrees that need CodeGraph under the home directory, as `<repo-parent>/<repo-name>-worktrees/<name>`, never under `/tmp` or `/var/tmp`; each worktree gets its own index, never a copied or linked one.
      - Never run `codegraph uninit`, `install`, `uninstall` or `upgrade`; run `codegraph index` only to recover a corrupt index. After edits rely on the watcher; run `codegraph sync` only when it is off or files stay stale.

      ## Remote operation authorization

      <CLAUDE.md:559-564 verbatim>
      ```
    - `output-styles/labdrian.md`: byte-identical to `~/.claude/output-styles/gentleman.md` except four changes: (1) frontmatter `name: labdrian` and `description: Direct, caring senior engineer: short answers, verifies before agreeing, teaches the why`; (2) the heading of line 7 becomes `# labdrian Output Style`; (3) line 23 drops Gentleman's own credentials and reads "Senior engineer and architect with long experience. Passionate teacher who genuinely wants people to learn and grow. Frustrated by shortcuts — because you know they can do better. Speak with energy, passion, and genuine desire to help."; (4) line 49 says "not from memory context" in place of "not from Engram or memory context". The Philosophy quotes (lines 69-72), Speech Patterns and every Language Rule stay as they are, voseo included.
  - Steelman: the owner's rules, voice and the skills the flow actually loads move into sources this repository owns and deploys; one rules file and one style feed both Claude (block and output style) and OpenCode (AGENTS.md, T14), so the runtimes cannot drift; dropping the `sdd-*` family removes about 4,700 lines that tell agents to use Engram for a pipeline no role runs; and T22 then has a closed list to remove, which makes S17's acceptance checkable.
  - Strongest counter-argument: T26 (b) turns `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, a file the owner hand-edits, into a partly generated file with an installer writer (Risk 1), when a block pasted once by hand in T22 would cost no code; and the `sdd-*` deletion is irreversible for any other project of the owner's that still runs `/sdd-*`, which this repository cannot see.
  - Recommendation: the generated block (one source for both runtimes, written by the install path T7 already proved, held by sandbox tests and a verifier), and the `sdd-*` drop only on the owner's answer (risk 2). Tradeoff accepted: one more installer writer under Risk 1, in exchange for a single source of the owner's rules that OpenCode's AGENTS.md also reads. Alternative considered: hand-maintained block, rejected because S13 needs a repository source for AGENTS.md anyway and two copies would drift (memory `hexagonal-clean-code-maximum.md`: prefer the decoupled option).
  - Units and sizing (sdd-time-estimation; basis `lines-ref n=7`, features landed 0.7-4.2x forecast, five of seven above 2x; minutes `no-actuals n=0`, low): T26 290-530 lines in two commits (40-120 min); T27 300-520 in two (40-120 min); T28 150-280 in one (30-90 min); T29 6,400-6,700 in three, about 6,300 of them deletions measured with Grep line counts for this entry (60-150 min). Every reasoned piece is under one ~400-line slice; T29 (a) 1,650-1,850 and (c) about 4,700 exceed it as pure deletions of whole files with their rows: splitting a file-set deletion adds review rounds and no reasoning, so the deviation is stated here instead (ODD's size heuristic is advisory; risk 7). S17's units add 890-1,490 reasoned lines to the feature, against L12's pre-inventory 800-2,500 across 3-7 units. T20 is a parallel prototyper and does not change these units except I11 and I31, which its result shapes later.

Recommendation: approve; the only voice change is line 23 (Gentleman's own credentials removed) and line 49 (Engram). Tradeoff: an approval now fixes wording T26 and T27 will pin in tests.
    2. "Do we drop the `sdd-*` skills and their `_shared` files, or rewrite them for memorycard?" L61 (2026-11-16) — T20 answered (prototyper, box 3 lookups + 1 spike, sandbox HOME and a disposable worktree, both removed). Q1 kill: labdrian does not need gentle-ai for `.atl/skill-registry.md` beyond its skills table: the table and header are written only by `gentle-ai skill-registry refresh` (the UserPromptSubmit hook in `~/.claude/settings.json`, ending `|| true`; `bin/lib/skill_registry.sh:77,149` wrap it); the scoped blocks (`engine propagate`), executor rows (`engine registry repair-sdd`, `engine/cmd/registry.go:88`) and the Hermes section (`engine registry refresh-hermes`, `engine/sddregistry/app/hermes.go:23`, which needs the `## Skills` and `## Loading protocol` headings) are labdrian`s. Without gentle-ai the hook silently does nothing and the table goes stale while `labdrian status --only registry` still passes (`engine/status/status.go:233`); `labdrian skill-registry refresh` and `repair-sdd-registry` (without `--no-refresh`) die with "gentle-ai not found on PATH". Q2 continue: no labdrian verb generates the table; a throwaway Go spike reproduced all 53 rows of the real registry byte for byte (after normalizing HOME and worktree paths; header and date not compared), and gentle-ai 4.2.0 in the sandbox as positive control matched them too. Rules measured: project dirs `skills`, `.claude/skills`, `.pi/skills`, `.agents/skills` first, then the user dirs in the order of the real "Sources scanned" list; key on frontmatter `name`; first source wins; skip `_shared`, `skill-registry` and `sdd-*`; sort by name; fold block-scalar descriptions with single spaces (`sddregistry.FrontmatterDescription`, `engine/sddregistry/sddregistry.go:94`, returns the literal `>` or `|`, so a real parse is needed). A new verb also needs the registry lock (`registryLockPath`), a skip outside a project, a cheap no-op path (the hook fires every prompt), preservation of the scoped blocks and executor rows, wiring through `engine/hookwire`, and rewording the `skill-registry` skill and `bin/lib/skill_registry.sh`. Forecast 150-300 lines, low confidence, `no-actuals`. Consequence for L60: rows I11 and I31 wait on a shaper entry that turns this into a task; if the generator is dropped instead, the registry freezes silently.

- **L62** (2026-11-16, shaper): L61 turned into a task, and the review findings on the L60/L61 commit answered. Adds T30; redesigns T28 (its after-clause and the refresh it names), T29 (commit (a) narrowed, three skills held back, (c) and the forecast recounted) and T22 (after-clause, held-back skills, the hook check), all three re-marked `shaped: L62`; T30 sits before T28 in Tasks because T28, T29 and T22 depend on it. No S# changes. Owner's words this design serves: L11 "tenemos que mejorar todo.lo que aprendemos.de gentle y dejar solo lo nusdtro adaptado y mejorado para nuestro flujo"; L13 "no vamos a correr mas gentle-ai sync".
  - T20 answered (L61), so L60's "T20 is a parallel prototyper" and "T20 decides" are settled: labdrian needs gentle-ai only for the skills table and header of `.atl/skill-registry.md`; no labdrian verb writes that table; a labdrian generator can replace it (a spike reproduced 53 of 53 rows). Decision taken here: build that generator as T30, the verb `engine registry refresh`, run by `labdrian skill-registry refresh` and by an owned UserPromptSubmit hook.
  - Method: Read, Grep and Glob only, on this worktree and, read-only, on the primary checkout `/home/labdrian/labdrian-sdd-overlay`. Not run by me: `git`, any test, gentle-ai.

    2. On this branch the only users of `engine/sddregistry` are the package itself and `engine/cmd/registry.go` with its tests (Grep; the `engine/runtime` hits are another `NewRegistry`); `FrontmatterDescription`'s one caller is `NewExecutor` (`sddregistry.go:77`). The executor list holds `sdd-time-estimation` (`sddregistry.go:52`), a labdrian skill that T29 keeps, so dropping the repair without changing the generator's `sdd-*` skip would leave it with no row.

T30 (d) wires the refresh there.

Design choices inside T30: (i) a hand-written frontmatter read, with no YAML dependency (Risk 5 avoided, evidence 3). A multi-line quoted description is read whole instead of cut at its first line, a stated deviation from gentle-ai pinned by a test, because a description cut mid-sentence feeds skill matching; this is the one place the rows differ from gentle's. (ii) A new hexagonal package `engine/skillregistry` with its own ports, writing through the existing `sddregistry/fsstore.Registry` and the existing lock, not a rename or move of `sddregistry`, which would collide with the Hermes branch editing the same package (memory `hexagonal-clean-code-maximum.md`). (iii) Every line the generator does not own is kept byte-identical, so the order in which `propagate`, `refresh-hermes` and `repair-sdd` run does not matter.

L61's 150-300 forecast covered the generator alone. T29 is recounted to 5,900-7,100 lines, 60-180 minutes. No other unit changes.
  - Order: T30 (after the risk-1 answer; not in parallel with T27 (b), since both edit `engine/settings`); then T28 after T30 (c); T29 after T28, T30 and the L60 risk-2 answer; T22 last, after T8.
