OpenCode gets no refresh hook (T11 bridges the projection hook only, S10), so on OpenCode the registry is refreshed by Claude sessions or the command; no S# asks for more. `labdrian status --only registry` passing over a stale table (L61) is not changed; no S# asks for it.
  - Risks (open; each is one question for the owner, with my recommendation; I decide none). L60 risks 1-6 stay open as written.
    1. "After T8 neither the foreman nor role-chain reads `.atl/skill-registry.md`: do we keep it current with a labdrian generator (T30), or retire the registry and its refresh?" Recommendation: keep (T30). Tradeoff: about 620-1,140 lines and a per-prompt hook, against a retirement that would touch `propagate`, `status` and the Hermes feature. A "keep" answer probably also needs a later shaper entry saying where the foreman takes skill paths from; that wording is not in its body today.
    2. "Do we drop `branch-pr`, `issue-creation` and `systemic-issue-triage`, or keep them as labdrian skills?" Recommendation: drop. They encode gentle-ai's `status:approved` issue-first policy and community triage (L60 I24-I26), and pull requests stay the owner's (S16 B10). Tradeoff: if the owner later wants an issue-first flow, it is rebuilt from T22's backup.

L78 (2026-11-16) — Owner, verbatim, after "no me gusta repetir errores" (the parent added a Learning section) and asked to approve the terse style with it: "aprieno" (read: "apruebo"). Approved text of `output-styles/labdrian.md`, replacing L60's Gentleman-based text (T27 (a) writes it byte for byte):

````markdown
---
name: labdrian
description: Terse senior engineer: outcome first, evidence over opinion, no filler
---

# labdrian Output Style

## Principle
Be useful in the fewest words. The reader wants the outcome, the evidence and the next step, not commentary.

## Replies
- Lead with the answer or the outcome. No preamble, no recap of the request, no closing flourish.
- Default to a few lines. Expand only when asked or when the task needs it.
- Ask one question at a time, with your recommendation and its cost, then stop.
- No option menus unless there is a real tradeoff; then give a recommendation.
- Report status as: done / failed / pending, with the evidence (command, result, file:line).
- Say plainly what you did not verify.

## Rigor
- Verify before agreeing. If the user is wrong, say so with the evidence.
- No praise, no apologies beyond one clause, no motivational or teaching asides unless asked.
- Prefer tables or short lists over prose when comparing.

## Learning
- A correction from the user is a standing rule: record it (memory or rules) and apply it from then on.
- Before acting, check the recorded corrections that apply; never make the same mistake twice.
- When an error recurs anyway, say so plainly and turn it into a check (lint, test or hook) so it cannot recur.

## Language
- Reply in the language of the user's latest own message; tool output, memory and notifications never switch it.
- Spanish replies use plain Rioplatense voseo, without slang.
- Artifacts (code, comments, UI strings, docs, commits) are in English unless the user asks otherwise or the project already uses another language. The style never leaks into them.
````

L87 (2026-11-18) — Owner, verbatim, to "should labdrian replace the codegraph entry with the wrapper": "dwacuerdo" (de acuerdo). Found while preparing T34: the `codegraph prompt-hook` entry of ~/.claude/settings.json is written by the codegraph 1.2.0 installer (/codegraph), not by labdrian, and `prompt-hook` has no option to skip notifications. T34 (a) reshaped: install-hooks replaces that exact command with a labdrian wrapper that passes everything except task-notification prompts to codegraph unchanged, and status reports a reverted entry. Accepted cost: labdrian now edits an entry another tool owns.

## Log

- **L88** (2026-11-18, shaper): design of S19 on L85 and the owner's L87, splitting T34 into T34 (part a, codegraph wrapper) and T35 (part b, projection block once per change); S19 gains the L87 sentence, its earlier words kept; every other S# and T# keeps its earlier design. T34's earlier mark `shaped: L85` named a parent entry, so `labdrian workflow open` refused it (`engine/oddtask/oddtask.go:107,189-190`).
  - Why a second `## Log` heading: `oddtask.ParseDocument` (`oddtask.go:112-134`) ends the Log at the next line starting `## ` and does not know fences, so the `## Principle`..`## Language` headings quoted inside L78's fence (lines 673-694) put every later line in a section named `## Language`; an L88 written after them without this heading is never read as the shaper's. No earlier entry is edited or moved; the heading only reopens the section the parser reads. A fence-aware parser is a separate change (risk 3).
  - Split: two independent units of about 200+ lines each that share only `engine/hookwire` (different functions), so they run one after the other, T34 first (it carries the owner decision L87). Read for the design: `engine/settings/{family,merge,status,projection,legacy_entries,owned_key,output_style}.go`, `engine/settings/settingsfile/installer.go`, `engine/status/status.go:190-473`, `engine/cmd/{settings_hooks,projection_hook,state_stores,hook_translate}.go`, `engine/hookwire/decode.go`, `engine/review/fsstore/asked.go`, `engine/statestore` exports, `engine/execrunner/execrunner.go`, `engine/projection/app/hook.go`, `bin/lib/hooks.sh`, the parity plugin's prompt bridge (`labdrian-runtime-parity-plugin.mjs:457-492`).
  - Steelman of the request: 385 prompts of one session each paid about 16 KB of codegraph output (2 KB reaching the model) and a repeated projection block, most of them task notifications (L85); both hooks answer the same thing again and again, so skipping the repeats saves context on every turn with no loss.
  - T34 design. The settings families (`hookFamily`, `family.go:16-24`) own whole entries by the binary path plus an identity token and append missing ones; the codegraph entry is foreign and must change in place, so a family does not fit: a small separate step in `mergeHooks`/`removeHooks` replaces one exact command string and gives it back on uninstall. The wrapper is an engine verb, not a shell `case` on the raw JSON: a shell match cannot tell the marker inside the prompt from the marker anywhere else in the input, and a prompt's JSON escapes can hide `<task-notification>`. Its `command -v ... || exec codegraph prompt-hook` keeps codegraph running when the engine binary is gone. Strongest counter-argument: labdrian now rewrites a value another installer owns, and a codegraph reinstall or upgrade silently puts the bare command back (or adds a second one), so the saving depends on someone re-running `install-hooks`. Accepted: the owner accepted that cost (L87); status warns when it happens and install removes a bare duplicate. Alternative rejected: a separate labdrian UserPromptSubmit entry cannot stop codegraph's entry from running, and editing codegraph's installer is outside this repository.
  - T35 design. The reply bytes are compared by hash per session; the store is append-only because `statestore.Publish` never replaces (`asked.go:32-33`), and the last line is the last block. Strongest counter-argument: after compaction or `/clear` the block is no longer in the model's context while the store still says it was sent, so an unchanged block would never come back. Mitigation in the design: the projection's SessionStart hook, which already fires on startup, resume, clear and compact (`projection.go:50-53`), records a reset. Tradeoff accepted: one small state directory per session, as the review stop-hook already keeps (`asked.go:27-34`), with no cleanup in this task.
  - Recommendation: build T34 then T35 as written.
  - Unverified: the exact text of a task-notification `prompt` in the hook's stdin (leading white space, the marker's position); T34's builder records it from a transcript. Whether OpenCode's `context` hook, bridged as SessionStart, fires once per session or per turn (L86 says it can insert the block mid-session); if per turn, T35 saves nothing on OpenCode and changes nothing there either. Codex has no UserPromptSubmit hook installed (`bin/lib/hooks.sh:45-48` installs SessionStart, PreToolUse, PostToolUse), so T35 does not reach it; read from the install text, not from a run.
  - Forecasts (sdd-time-estimation): T34 pieces: verb, wire reader, settings step, status check, notice, docs, tests; 420-760 lines in two commits, because T26 (b) landed about 1,130 against 200-360 (L79); T35 pieces: decoder fields, store, command logic, SessionStart reset, tests; 220-400 lines. Minutes are agent compute time, `no-actuals n=0`, low.
  - Risks: (1) T34: Risk items 1, 3 and 5, independent verifier after (a2). (2) T35: items 3 and 5, independent verifier after it. (3) The parser ignores fences (this entry's heading is the workaround); a fence-aware `ParseDocument` is not asked for and is left to the owner. No open product decision.

  - B1. Cause, from the code: `cmd_apply` runs a runtime install only for codex (`apply.sh:190-194`) and pi (`:121-122`); for opencode it copies the manifest rows (agents) and nothing writes the plugins, `opencode.json` or AGENTS.md; the longterm-mem step that follows reads `<config>/opencode.json` and fails on a missing file (`longterm-mem/internal/register/opencode.go:95-97`; the exact L99 message is not in the Log, so T37 reproduces it first). Design (T37): one runtime-install step taking the target, T31's function generalized rather than copied. "Target state records a successful deploy": the row is the manifest digest of what was copied (`apply.sh:183-188`), and a register failure after it already makes apply exit non-zero by design (D13, `:197-208,234`); with the root cause gone the register succeeds, so the state semantics stay. Strongest counter-argument: the state row can still say "deployed" for opencode while the MCP step failed, which is exactly what the verifier objected to. Accepted: changing when every target records state is a shared-contract change (Risk 3) for claude and codex that L99 did not show broken; apply's exit code carries the failure.
  - B2. Evidence from the 2.2.7 binary: skill discovery resolves `home/.claude` and `home/.agents` with `home` read as `process.env.OPENCODE_TEST_HOME??E.homedir()`, and also walks up from the session directory collecting `.claude`, `.agents` and `.opencode` directories (`r.up({targets:["."],start:s.directory})`, `T=[".claude",".agents",".opencode",...]`); `.claude` is skipped only under `OPENCODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_CODE` or `OPENCODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_CODE_SKILLS`, `.agents` has no switch. L99's sandbox `/home/labdrian/.cache/t17.fT4y` lies under `/home/labdrian`, so the walk-up alone reaches the live `~/.claude` and `~/.agents`, whatever `HOME` is; whether Bun's `homedir()` also ignores `HOME` is unverified (T36 premise 3). Answer to the parent's question: for isolation it is an acceptance problem only: live OpenCode is meant to read the deployed `~/.claude/skills` (S13, L10 T5), so the overlay deploys no second copy. But it also reaches the live switch: the live `~/.agents/skills` holds 16 skills, among them `branch-pr`, `issue-creation`, `chained-pr`, `work-unit-commits`, `skill-registry` and `go-testing`, which by L10 T5's precedence shadow the `~/.claude/skills` copies, so live OpenCode would load the pre-T32/T33 `branch-pr` and `issue-creation`. S13 already has the install report them and leaves their removal to the owner (`opencode_agents.go:152-196`), so no code task: S15 now makes that report empty a precondition of proposing the switch.   - B3. Two observations conflict: L10 T2 measured `execute.before` firing for inner MCP calls under Code Mode; L99 saw the memorycard read only as `execute`. Options weighed. (a) Map `execute` through the gate: the gate judges a read by its tool name and its `project` argument (`gate.go:310-330`; the deny text this session got from Claude Code: "this memorycard read names no project ..."), but under Code Mode those live inside model-written JavaScript, which the gate would have to evaluate; static reading cannot know a computed `project`, so the gate would either deny every `execute` that touches a memory server (Code Mode off in effect, with worse messages) or let reads through unjudged. (b) Code Mode off for every server: simple, but loses Code Mode for codegraph, whose large results Code Mode lets the model filter in code, and no gate needs codegraph. (c) Code Mode off only for memorycard and longterm-mem, the servers the gate judges, on for codegraph. Recommended: (c), after T36 settles which observation holds (premise 1); if the inner call does carry `memorycard_<tool>` and its arguments under Code Mode, the fix is in the mapping and T38 is reshaped. Cost of (c), found while designing it: under Code Mode a role that keeps `execute` reaches every memory tool through it (the generated `opencode/agents/polisher.md` denies no `execute`, so the polisher, whose Claude list holds only `mem_search` and `mem_get`, can call `mem_save` by code), while the generated `opencode/agents/reviewer.md` denies `execute` and so loses even its `mem_get`; with the servers out of Code Mode each tool is a direct tool every agent holds unless denied, so S7's "match the Claude tool lists" now needs per-tool denies (T39), whose rule form (pattern or exact name, rule order) L10 T4 never measured ("Not measured: resource-pattern granularity"), hence T36 premise 2. Strongest counter-argument: two Code Mode settings in one file plus per-tool permission lists is more configuration to keep in step, and every MCP tool definition of the two memory servers now sits in each session's context. Accepted: S15 asks that "the memory gate answers as it does in Claude Code", and the denies also close a least-privilege gap Code Mode hid.
  - B4. How Claude Code does it: the parent pastes the diff into the reviewer prompt (`skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:34`); Codex gets it from the engine, which appends the -U3 diff and changed paths itself (`review launch`, `engine/review/app/launch.go:134-154`). Options: (a) the OpenCode foreman pastes it, as in Claude Code: no code, but a GPT foreman must re-emit up to 150 KB verbatim as tool-call arguments (output tokens, no byte check), and L99 shows it did not; (b) a read-only diff file the reviewer reads: needs a second path in the reviewer's "read nothing outside that path" rule and a file outside the bound worktree that nothing binds to `diff_sha256`, and L99's copy into the worktree changed the tree under review; (c) the parity plugin, which already rewrites `subagent` prompts (S8), has the engine append the frozen context to a reviewer launch, the same bytes Codex gets. Recommended: (c) (T40 engine verb, T41 plugin), split because together they exceed one slice. Strongest counter-argument: the three runtimes now deliver the diff three ways, and the plugin gains a reviewer-specific branch keyed on an agent name. Accepted: the bytes come from one engine function shared with Codex, the reviewer contract is unchanged, and role-chain already branches per runtime (`:36`). Fail closed: a reviewer launch the verb refuses or cannot answer is denied, because a review without its diff is worthless and would still be recorded against the binding.

  - Steelman of the request: L115 met every S15 item the plugin, the permissions and the frozen context carry; what is left is one design gap (B5: a role on OpenCode cannot reach the skills its own body names) and four observation gaps (gate deny, context, capture, Tier 3) plus closure. Each gap has a fix that does not depend on a model behaving: B5 and the gate deny move into engine code and tests, and the observation gaps become fixed probes, so the next re-run measures the harness rather than the model's willingness.
  - B5, decision. Options weighed. (1) Allow the `skill` tool to the eight roles on OpenCode: one permission per role and native loading; but it breaks S7's rule of matching the Claude tool lists (no Claude role holds `Skill`, `roles.go:69-148`), it fixes nothing on the other runtimes, and L100 kept the deny on purpose (A5). (2) Prose: role-chain or the foreman body tells the parent to name absolute paths: the gentle-ai Skill Registry Protocol was exactly that prose and L113 shows a GPT foreman not following an equivalent rule ("## Skills to load before work: test-first, go-test"). (3) Chosen: (a) the skills a body names reach the role through the contract injection that already works on OpenCode (L115: "roles receive the injected contract"), fed by an engine function and guarded by a generator check; (b) the skills a handoff lists are resolved by an engine verb that refuses what it cannot resolve, with a message telling the foreman what to write. (a) adds no plugin code: the adapter writes entries in the contract shape the plugin already applies. Strongest counter-argument: (a) reads prose: a body that names a skill in another phrasing (not `` `<id>` skill``) gets no path and no error; and (b) makes a launch fail where it went through before, so a foreman that writes a free-form skill list is stopped. Accepted: every skill mention in the nine bodies today has that form (Grep), the generator check keeps each one resolvable, and a refused launch returns the fix to the foreman in one line, which is the layer-2 behavior the brief asks for; a reviewer brief is checked before its diff is appended, so a diff that holds the header is never read as a skill list. Paths point at the overlay checkout, as the contracts' do (eafc1015), not at the deployed `~/.claude/skills`: an `apply --target opencode` on a home with no Claude skills (T37's empty-root test) still installs. Tradeoff accepted: a role reads the checkout's text, which can be ahead of the deployed, owner-approved copy, as the contracts already do.
  - B5, per runtime (from the code, S8 L117): OpenCode gets (a) and (b). Claude Code, Codex and Pi get only the generator check: Claude's `gate-task` injects only the contracts (`engine/cmd/contracts.go:15-18`) and is fail-safe by contract ("It must NEVER block an Agent call, NEVER crash, NEVER deny", `gate.go:23-25`); Codex has no engine-installed launch hook (`codexhooks/document.go:36-37`); Pi neither. Observed on Claude Code in this very launch: the brief named `odd-feature-doc` with no path and this shaper found it with Glob.

No skill text changes, so no owner re-approval is needed for T42-T45.
  - Question for the owner (closure, the one fork only the owner settles): "For a repository with no CI, should the foreman close a workflow on your landing commit plus the delivery's recorded local test results, written as `CI: none configured` (recommended), or must the T17 re-run land through a real GitHub repository with CI, which needs your authorization for a remote sandbox repository?" Recommendation: the no-CI path, because the gate then says honestly what was checked and works for every local repository; tradeoff: a repository whose CI the foreman cannot see (none under `.github/workflows`, another provider) would close on local results, so the rule must name how "no CI" is established; the shaper designs that rule after the answer.

L121 (2026-11-18) — Owner, verbatim: "quiero mantener la regla del ci" — answers L117's closure question: the foreman's Close row (roles/foreman/body.md:29, "the merge commit, and CI read green") stays as it is; no no-CI closure path. L122 (2026-11-18) — Owner, verbatim: "pero nada vamos con la 1" — replaces L121. "la 1" is option 1 of the parent's closure question: "Agregar un camino para repos sin CI (lo que recomiendo). El foreman cierra con el commit de landing más los tests locales que registró delivery, y lo anota como `CI: none configured`. Sirve para el sandbox y para cualquier proyecto tuyo que no tenga CI. La contra es que hay que definir cómo se establece que un repo "no tiene CI", para que no sirva de excusa cuando el CI existe y falla." No remote repository is used for T17. Shaper re-entry launched to design the no-CI closure path.

- **L123** (2026-11-18, shaper): design of the no-CI closure path on L122 ("pero nada vamos con la 1"), which replaces L121 ("quiero mantener la regla del ci"), under the L116 principle (engine code and a test first, prose only for judgment). Amends S3 and S15 (earlier text kept, the owner's words quoted); adds T46 and T47 (IDs after T45, the highest); amends T17's dependency and closure check, so its mark moves from L117 to this entry. Every other S# and T# keeps its earlier design.
  - Read for the design: this document to `## Log`, L114-L122; `roles/foreman/body.md` (whole), `roles/delivery/body.md` (whole); `engine/cmd/workflow.go:365-431` (`workflow verify`, `workflow close`); `engine/workflow/lifecycle.go:140-180,760-860`; `engine/workflow/workflow.go:146-209` and the Grep of `Outcome`/`Reason` (`:831-844`); `engine/cmd/review.go:72-90,523-617` (`review check`, `reviewFailed`); `engine/review/app/app.go:100-149` (the git port); `engine/review/gitexec/gitexec.go:586-610,990-1035`; `skills/inception-pipeline/references/closure-feedback.md:1-30`; `skills/odd-feature-doc/SKILL.md:37` (foreman row); `engine/agentgen/foreman_test.go:215-254`; `engine/skills/role_agents_artifact_test.go:800-823`; `engine/cmd/usage.go` (Grep). CodeGraph not used: no `.codegraph/` in the worktree (L117) and no shell here. Nothing written outside this document; the memorycard mirror is the parent's.
  - Steelman of option 1: the Close row today demands evidence a repository with no CI cannot produce (L114: "Record CI as unavailable. That does not satisfy the merge or closure gate"), so every local project stays open forever, and the foreman was right to refuse. `CI: none configured` replaces an impossible proof with an honest one, and if the engine, not the model, decides that a repository has no CI, the excuse the owner's option warns about ("para que no sirva de excusa cuando el CI existe y falla") cannot come from a model's reading of the situation.
  - Where the establishment lives, options weighed. (1) A field or flag of `workflow close`: rejected. A completed close must carry an empty reason (`workflow.go:833-834`), so recording the finding there changes the persisted event format (Risk 1); the workflow package runs no git or other subprocess by design (`lifecycle.go:170-176`); and the CI-green half cannot be checked by the engine without the network, so the gate would be half engine anyway. (2) `workflow verify`: rejected for the same package reason; it checks the event chain, goal and role chain only (`lifecycle.go:760-830`). (3) Chosen: a read-only `review ci` verb in `engine/review`, whose git port already has every read but one (`TreeFiles` `app.go:129-131`, `IsAncestor` `:144-146`, `Commit` `gitexec.go:198`; new: `Remotes`), shaped as `review check` (exit 0 with stdout, refusal non-zero with one stderr line, `reviewFailed`, `review.go:609-617`). It records nothing: the finding is reproducible from the commit and the remotes, and the record is versioned prose, as closure already records its landing anchor ("closure records the anchor in versioned prose; it is never discovered from unversioned state", `closure-feedback.md:27`): the foreman's closure report quotes the line, and delivery's entry holds the tests.
  - The rule ("no CI" established): the landing holds the delivered head; no `git remote`; no CI marker in the landing commit's tree (the S3 L123 list, an assumption). The remote rule carries the weight: a hosted CI runs only on pushed commits, so with no remote none of them can have run. The marker list guards the rest: a repository whose remote was removed, or a local runner (`Jenkinsfile`, `.github/workflows` run locally). The tree of the landing commit is read, never the working tree, so deleting a workflow file locally changes nothing, and a commit that deletes one is in history for review.
  - Strongest counter-argument to the chosen design: both signals are heuristics. A provider outside the list, run by a local runner, is excused; and delivery's tests run at the delivered head, not at the landing tree, so a merge that brings other changes closes on tests of a tree that never existed alone. Accepted: with no remote, the only CI left is local, which the list covers for the common providers; the landing must hold the head, and the foreman already re-runs one reported command before advancing (`roles/foreman/body.md:9`, S16 B4). Tradeoff accepted: a repository that has a remote and no CI marker (a GitHub repository with no workflows) cannot use the path and cannot close either (risk 2).
  - What the foreman says at closure: its reply's Evidence line (S16 B7) names the landing commit, quotes the verb's stdout line, which starts `CI: none configured`, and names delivery's test command and result with its Log entry; `labdrian workflow close --outcome completed` then runs as before.
  - Per runtime (the engine is shared): the verb runs wherever `labdrian` runs. The foreman exists only on Claude Code (`agents/foreman.md`) and OpenCode (`opencode/agents/foreman.md`) (S5), so the no-CI Close row reaches those two. The delivery sentence reaches all four through the generated `agents/delivery.md`, `opencode/agents/delivery.md`, `codex/agents/delivery.toml` and `pi/agents/delivery.md`; on Codex and Pi, where no foreman closes, delivery records the local test results and nothing reads `review ci` unless their parent runs it. No hook, plugin or permission changes on any runtime.
  - Order: T43 (running) → T46 (after T43's commit (b): both add a verb and edit `engine/cmd/usage.go` and README) → T47 (after T46: the foreman body's verb test needs `review ci` to exist) → the one independent verifier, extended from T42, T43 and T45 (L117) to also cover T46 (Risk 2) and T47, with the whole document, launched after T47 instead of after T43 (b), so one verifier covers all five; if the parent keeps it after T43 (b), T46 needs its own verifier → T17's re-run. Forecasts (sdd-time-estimation, this repository's reference class): T46 240-400, T47 40-100; total 280-500 authored lines (basis `lines-ref n=7`; T46 low, T47 medium); minutes in each task line (`no-actuals n=0`, low). No skill text changes, so no owner re-approval.
  - Risks, each the owner's decision, none blocking T46, T47 or T17: (1) landing in a no-CI repository: the foreman's Never line "Never merge, or arm anything that merges, before reading `gh pr checks` with no failing check and the review recorded clear" (`roles/foreman/body.md:36`) and S16 B10 ("a merge is preceded by `gh pr checks` with no failing line") still stop the foreman from making a local merge the owner asks for (as in L114), so in T17 the landing is a hand step outside the foreman session; recommendation: in a later re-entry, let `review ci --landing <head> --head <head>` exiting 0 stand in for `gh pr checks` before an owner-asked local merge, since the head's tree is the one being merged; tradeoff: the foreman then runs a merge, an owner action, on the owner's message alone. (2) A repository with a remote and no CI marker is refused (`CI: not established`), so it cannot close on either path; recommendation: keep refusing in this feature (T17's sandbox has no remote, L122) and weigh, if a real repository hits it, reading the host's checks for the landing commit (`gh`, network) rather than trusting the absence of files; tradeoff: such repositories stay open until then. (3) Delivery records local test results on every delivery, CI or not (it cannot know whether the landing will have CI); recommendation: accept; tradeoff: one suite run per delivery in repositories that also have CI. (4) The marker list is an assumption (S3 L123); recommendation: accept it as the closed list T46 pins, extended by a test when a new provider appears.
  - Unverified: that the sandbox's landing will be a commit the T17 foreman is told in its prompt (the hand-run decides); that `git remote` in a worktree lists the remotes of its main repository (git shares the config; T46's test can pin it if the parent wants).

- **L144** (2026-11-19, shaper) — T53 design (T51's C3, W8), from L143 and the code read at 284962ad: roles/delivery/body.md:9 (the test-runner sentence allows "not run"), roles/foreman/body.md:29 (the Close cell accepts "the local test results in delivery's entry" whatever they say), engine/cmd/review_ci.go:14-39 and engine/review/app/ci.go:20-58 (`CheckNoCI`), engine/review/app/app.go:144 (`IsAncestor` true for "b itself"), engine/agentgen/foreman_test.go:258-275 and engine/skills/role_agents_artifact_test.go:1119-1129 (the pinned texts). S3 and S15 amended with L143's words. Design: delivery predicts no CI with `review ci --landing <head> --head <head>` before the landing exists (the landing is the owner's, S15 L123), the same verb the foreman runs at Close on the real landing; when it exits 0, a test run at the delivered head that passed is required, else delivery closes nothing and returns blocked; when it exits non-zero, today's sentence stands. The foreman's Close cell requires a passing run in the entry; "not run" or a failure is missing evidence (Discipline: name the missing evidence). Steelman of the alternative (delivery always must run the tests at the head and pass, no `review ci` call): one rule, no prediction, stronger evidence everywhere. Counter-argument to the chosen design: delivery's prediction reads the head's tree, not the landing's; a landing that adds a CI marker or a remote added after delivery makes the two answers differ. Accepted tradeoff: the foreman's Close check on the real landing stays authoritative and fails closed (a no-CI landing with a "not run" entry cannot close), and repositories with CI keep today's delivery behavior (no unasked change to the CI path). RED: the new `want` of `TestTheCloseRowAcceptsTheNoCIFindingBesideGreenCI` and the new `TestDeliveryRequiresAPassingRunAtTheHeadWhenNoCI` fail on the current bodies. - **L148** (2026-11-19, shaper) — T50 design: the review stop hook and a branch made from another branch. Scope: the second half of the old T50 line (1d0aabf's trailer `Risk: 3 (agentgen projection shared by four runtimes, pinned byte-identical)`) is resolved by T52 (e5ffe5ee, 284962ad, L142: a parenthesized reason after the Risk value is read, pushed history kept) and is dropped from T50. Evidence: T8 transcript line 95, the stop-hook block "Commit 1d0aabf772101c18bc45330574a0420340671db4 of branch feat/t8-gadu-opencode-message has unfinished review steps. Before you stop, for that commit alone (base b35a55b868da5d3efcc422386433f52ec6c0c29e)", on a branch made at 99290fbc, the head of feat/labdrian-orchestrator-opencode, with no commit of its own (L133); later in the run the owner said "Sí, entregalo apilado sobre feat/labdrian-orchestrator-opencode." (line 699), and the parent's spot check ran `review check --onto feat/labdrian-orchestrator-opencode --head 7154b2f6` (L135). Cause, at e42c41c5: the hook's question is `NextUnreviewed` (`engine/review/app/unreviewed.go:59-130`, called by `Stop`, `stop.go:45-66`), whose commits are `Standing.Commits`: `git rev-list --first-parent --reverse <HEAD> ^<tip>...` with the tips of the default branch only, local and origin (`engine/review/gitexec/gitexec.go:401-446`; `defaultBranch` :1011-1035: origin/HEAD, else main, else master). A branch made from a branch other than the default one therefore owns, for the hook, every commit of that branch that main lacks, and the first that misses anything is asked (unreviewed.go:111-127); at the T8 run 1d0aabf had the trailer T52 later accepted and the 14 commits 1d0aabf7..99290fbc had no review (L138). The hook was right that those commits missed records; it asked the wrong session. Options. (A, recommended) the branch's upstream bounds it: when HEAD's branch tracks a branch of another name, the commits that upstream holds are not the branch's own. (B) leave out every commit any other branch holds (`rev-list ... --exclude=<branch> --branches --remotes`). (C) keep the range and say in the reason which other branches hold the commit. (D) no change. Steelman of D: the hook was correct; inherited commits reach main through the stacked branch too, the question is asked once per session and commit (`stop.go:40-44`), and after the backfill (L139-L141) and T52 nothing is asked today; any narrowing weakens a guard. Against B: it fails open: a backup branch made at HEAD hides every commit of the branch. Against C and D: in a feature branch chain (`skills/chained-pr/references/chaining-details.md:12-33`, a strategy this feature's header names) every child session is asked about the tracker's commits, which its own pull request, checked with `review check --onto <parent>` (`engine/review/app/onto.go:12,37-41`), does not hold. Strongest counter-argument to A: it narrows what a guard asks; a parent commit missing a record is then asked only in the parent branch's sessions and caught by the parent's `review check --onto`; and a branch made without `--track` keeps today's range, so A alone would not have silenced the T8 question (the T8 branch's config was not read: unverified whether it had an upstream). Accepted tradeoff: it fails closed: no upstream, an upstream of the branch's own name (as after `git push -u`), or an upstream that cannot be read keep today's range. Design (A). In `Standing`, after `defaultBranch` gives a name: skip when the branch name holds `*`, `?`, `[`, `\` or a newline (the guard `defaultBranch` applies, :1017); read the ref the branch tracks with `git for-each-ref --format=%(upstream) refs/heads/<branch>` and the name it merges with `git config --get branch.<branch>.merge` (exit 1 with no output is "no upstream"); when the merge name is `refs/heads/<P>` with P different from the branch and the tracked ref has one tip (`for-each-ref --format=%(objectname) <ref>` through `commitNames`), add `^<tip>` to the `rev-list` arguments; any error or other answer of these reads adds nothing and is not returned, so `Standing` fails as before or not at all and `review proposals`, which reads only `Branch` and `Default` (`proposals.go:16-57`), is unchanged. The builder confirms in the RED fixtures what `%(upstream)` prints for a local upstream (`refs/heads/<P>`), a remote one (`refs/remotes/<r>/<P>`) and a missing branch. Docs: `engine/cmd/usage.go:313` and `README.md:1513-1516` say the commits are "those of the branch that the default branch lacks and, when the branch tracks a branch of another name (its upstream), that branch lacks too"; `skills/chained-pr/references/chaining-details.md` steps 3 and 4 create each child with `git switch -c <child> --track <parent>`, so the stop hook asks only for the child's commits. Spec text for S3 on a yes to R1 (a shaper re-entry pastes it): "Amended in L148: the review stop hook asks a session only for the commits of its branch that the default branch lacks and, when the branch tracks a branch of another name, that branch lacks too; with no upstream, or one of the branch's own name, it asks as before." RED: the two upstream rows of `TestStandingSaysWhereHeadStandsInItsBranch` and the stacked case of `TestStopHookDoesNotAskAStackedBranchForItsParentsCommits` fail at e42c41c5. Forecast 130-220 lines (`lines-ref n=7`; low), 40-120 minutes (`no-actuals n=0`, low). Risks. R1 (owner decision, the one question): "On a branch stacked on another branch that is not main, should the review stop hook stop asking the session for the commits it inherited from that branch, when the stacked branch tracks it as its upstream?" Recommendation: yes (A). Tradeoff: those commits are then asked only in the parent branch's sessions and checked by its `review check --onto`; a branch made without `--track` keeps today's behavior. No work on T50 before the answer. R2 (advisory): if `skills/chained-pr/.approval.json` hashes the reference file, the doc edit voids the approval and its re-approval is the owner's. R3 (advisory, unverified): `git push -u` moves the upstream to the branch's own remote name, after which the hook asks for the parent's commits again.

  - Steelman of the request: S17 says adopted assets are "deployed by `labdrian apply`"; codex (T31) and opencode (T37) already get their owner-rules block from apply through one shared step, so Claude is the only runtime whose block needs a hand-run engine command, and L149 shows the live machine left partial by exactly that gap.

(A, chosen) a narrow engine entry: `engine runtime <action> --component owner-rules --target claude`, an adapter over the owner-rules half of the Claude adapter only, called by `runtime_install_and_report claude`. (B) apply runs the full `runtime install --target claude` and `usage.sh:526` changes. (C) a new top-level engine verb for the block. Steelman of B: no new CLI surface, about 30 lines; the adapter's hook command `<claude root>/bin/gentle-ai-overlay` (`claude.go:204`) is the `ENGINE_BINARY` `install-hooks` passes, and `Installer.Install` writes nothing when nothing changed (`installer.go:31-33`), so on a machine that already ran `install-hooks` B changes no byte of `settings.json`. Against B: `ClaudeAdapter.Install` calls `hooks.Install` before the block (`claude.go:47-52`), and `mergeHooks` writes every hook family, the `permissions.deny` clearance rule, `"agent": "foreman"`, `"outputStyle": "labdrian"` and the codegraph wrapper (`merge.go:57-80`); on any machine where `install-hooks` was not run (a fresh setup, a sandbox, a CI clone) a deploy command would switch the foreman main session, the output style and every hook on, overturning hooks.sh's decisions 1 and 2 and `usage.sh:526-528`, an owner-facing contract nobody asked to change. Against C: a verb outside the `runtime` vocabulary would need its own flags and exit rules, and `runtime_install_and_report` could not call it without a second command shape. Strongest counter-argument to A: it adds a third way to write one block (runtime-parity install, the component, and the Codex adapter's own) and stretches `--component`, whose only other value is "a single component spanning claude+opencode+codex" (`usage.go:41-42`, `runtime.go:105-106`), to a slice of one runtime's adapter; and B would have fixed the live machine with no new vocabulary. Accepted tradeoff: the component's adapter holds no `HookInstaller`, so it cannot reach `settings.json` by construction (the port boundary, not a flag, enforces the separation); the owner-rules logic moves once into one type both Claude adapters delegate to, never copied, with every existing message byte-identical; and apply keeps one step shape (`runtime install --target <t>` plus arguments chosen in the step's `case`).
  - Placement: after the state row, as codex (the block is not a manifest row and the target is deployed without it); not before it as opencode, whose longterm-mem register needs `opencode.json`. Output change, `apply --target claude` (and `all`): one indented engine line after `  claude: deployed=N in-sync=M`, for example `  [claude] install: <status> — owner rules written to <home>/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (status text and wording as `result.String()` prints them, `lifecycle.go:58-69`; the builder records the exact line); a checkout without `instructions/owner-rules.md` prints nothing more; no engine binary prints the note of `apply.sh:44`; a failure goes to stderr and makes apply exit non-zero. `apply --target codex`, `opencode`, `pi` and every runtime-parity `runtime` call are unchanged (differential on the T54 line); `engine --help` and the unknown-component error gain the value.
  - RED: `runtime_install_and_report claude` returns 1 (`apply.sh:40`); `--component owner-rules` is refused as unknown (`runtime.go:194`); the end-to-end apply finds no block in CLAUDE.md.

Pieces (b): the step case and call, apply comments, two shell test files, usage, README, CHANGELOG: 120-240 lines. Total 300-560 (`lines-ref n=7`; widened because installer units of this feature landed over 2x, L79; low); minutes 90-240 (`no-actuals n=0`, low). Two commits, each under one slice.

R4 (advisory, unverified): the live machine already holds a current block (L149's hand-run install), so the first apply after the merge should print "already current"; observed only when the owner applies. R5 (Risk items): (a) 3, (b) 1, 3 and 5, independent verifier after (b). No open product decision.
